WESTPAC ECONOMICS
further 2.3%mth fall following a 1.9%mth decline in August,
and spending on apparel replicated the 2.0%mth decline
reported in August. Spending on durable goods also fell
slightly in September (-0.4%mth).
Despite the weak outcome for the August and September

months, retail spending over the September quarter still
eked out a minor 0.1% gain, while core spending increased
0.4%. Given higher prices, at face value this points to a weak
quarter for volumes (next week’s CPI report will cast more
light on pricing behaviour during the quarter). It is worth
noting that total retail spending, as will be depicted in the
official quarterly retail sales survey released next month, could
point to somewhat stronger growth. Purchases of big-ticket

items that often are bought on credit – such as cars, furniture
and appliances – are underrepresented in the electronic card
spending survey.
Given subdued domestic demand, local hospitality businesses
have relied on foreign visitors to drive growth in spending.
And the good news is that visitor arrivals increased to a new
post-Covid high in August to be up 8%yr. Arrivals of Chinese
citizens have grown by almost a third to sit near pre-Covid
levels in recent months, while arrivals of US citizens grew

17%yr. However, the Middle East conflict has impacted
long-haul arrivals from Europe (UK arrivals are down 8%yr).
Meanwhile, inflows of migrants remain at modest levels,
totalling around 20,000 in the year to August – about half
what might be expected on average over the cycle.
News from the manufacturing sector remained positive in
September, with the BusinessNZ Manufacturing PMI rising
to a 3-month high of 56.5 in September. Improvement was
seen across all subcomponents of the index, with the new

orders index rising 1.8pts to 57.0. We think that the recovery in
manufacturing this year owes much to the weakness of the NZ
dollar – especially against the Australian dollar – and activity
associated with a strong domestic primary sector and an
emerging recovery in domestic residential construction activity.
Last but certainly not least, ahead of next week’s all-important
Q3 CPI, the Selected Prices report for September was firmer
than expected. That was mainly due to larger than expected

increases in airfares and holiday accommodation costs, which
offset softer than expected food prices.
Accounting for the September prices data, we’ve left our
forecast for Q3 inflation  at 0.7%. That would see the annual
inflation rate dropping back to 3.7%, down from 4.1% in the
year to June. Underlying that move has been a moderation in
fuel prices after they rocketed higher in the wake of the Middle
East war. That’s balanced against increases in council rates,

building costs and food prices.
Our forecast is lower than the RBNZ’s September MPS
forecast for a 0.8% rise (+3.9%yr). However, with some large
swings in volatile items like fuel, the RBNZ will likely focus
on measures of core inflation. Core inflation was already at
firm levels in the June quarter, with key measures sitting in
the upper part of the RBNZ’s target band. We expect that
they will take another step higher in September, to sit around

3%. Upside surprises in core inflation would be an important
development for the RBNZ ahead of the October OCR review,
as this could suggest that the lower NZD and higher oil prices
are boosting underlying price pressures by more and sooner
than they may have expected.

### Page 7

Q3 is likely to produce a very similar outcome to Q2, with 
quarterly growth of around 1.0% holding the annual rate at 
4.3%yr. Year-to-date, growth will remain around 4.7%ytd 
thanks to the Q1 result.
The detail and partial data for September will again highlight 
the weak state of the consumer and domestic-oriented 
businesses, but also the incredible strength of exports. With 
the trade surplus back at record highs, there are limits to the 
contribution possible from net exports hence, necessitating 
a strengthening of domestic demand if aggregate growth is 
to continue at the rate authorities intend into 2027. Expect 
additional stimulus through the turn of the year.

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -30.55 điểm tương ứng -1.68%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Dệt may, Tiện ích khí và Bao bì & đóng gói là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt 
nhất trong tuần. Khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị  
-2,865 tỷ đồng, tập trung ở các mã VHM, VPB và VIC; đồng thời, tự doanh đảo chiều bán 
ròng ở mức -146 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại bán ròng -3,351 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua nhưng 
đang duy trì vị thế mua lũy kế 1,650 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 2,822 hợp đồng nhưng 
đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -6,636 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam bước vào tuần đầu tiên ở vị thế thị trường mới nổi bằng một nhịp điều 
chỉnh, khi VN-Index để mất mốc 1.800 điểm và lùi về đóng cửa quanh 1.785 điểm. Đây là 
phản ứng "mua kỳ vọng, bán thực tế" khá điển hình: ngay trong phiên nâng hạng chính 
thức 21/9, chỉ số mở cửa hưng phấn rồi nhanh chóng đảo chiều do áp lực chốt lời, với 
nhóm cổ phiếu Vingroup (VIC, VHM, VRE) giảm mạnh dẫn dắt đà đi xuống, và sức ép lan 
rộng sang bất động sản, ngân hàng, năng lượng về cuối tuần. Điểm đáng chú ý là dòng 
vốn ngoại đã tiếp tục bán ròng ngay sau cột mốc ngày 21/9, dù đã mua ròng trở lại trong 
tuần trước đó. Bối cảnh bên ngoài cũng kém thuận lợi khi Fed vừa tăng lãi suất và phát đi 
tín hiệu diều hâu, chứng khoán Mỹ điều chỉnh, giá dầu neo cao, và lợi suất trái phiếu 
Chính phủ Mỹ tăng vọt. Trong một, hai tuần tới, bên cạnh các yếu tố ngoại biên, mùa báo 
cáo KQKD quý 3 cũng sẽ bắt đầu tác động lên diễn biến thị trường chung.

Lợi nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ tăng 
trưởng khả quan trong 2Q26 14% 
YoY, với động lực chính đến từ mảng 
dịch vụ CNTT nước ngoài

Mảng CNTT nước ngoài: Lĩnh vực dịch 
vụ IT phân cực mạnh mẽ do tác nhân 
AI (AI Agentic) s ẽ củng c ố xu hư ớng 
đầu tư vào AI

FPT ở vị thế thuận lợi cho phép 
doanh nghiệp đa dạng hóa công cụ 
AI và tối ưu chi phí vận hành

Khuyến ngh ị MUA với giá m ục tiêu 
86,000 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Do không còn h ạch toán FPT Telecom (FOX) là công ty con, d oanh thu 2Q2026 của 
FPT giảm mạnh còn 13,810 tỷ VND (-17% YoY). Lợi nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ không 
bị ảnh hưởng bởi thay đổi hạch toán đạt 2,568 tỷ VND (14% YoY). Mảng dịch vụ CNTT 
tại thị trường nước ngoài tiếp tục là đ ộng lực tăng trư ởng chính, đạt doanh thu và 
LNTT lần lượt là 9,868/1,538 tỷ VND (16.3% /19.3% YoY).

Sự chuyển dịch chi tiêu từ dịch vụ IT truyền thống sang chuyển đổi số và tích hợp AI 
được kỳ vọng sẽ diễn ra ngày m ột mạnh mẽ khi: i) Sự phát triển của năng l ực tính 
toán giúp kéo giảm chi phí token (đơn v ị năng lực tính toán của AI) và thúc đ ẩy nhu 
cầu sử dụng AI của doanh nghiệp; ii) Các mô hình AI mã nguồn mở gia tăng lựa chọn 
cho người dùng sẽ thúc đẩy chi tiêu

Đối với các doanh nghiệp ứng dụng sâu các mô hình AI phục vụ hoạt động kinh doanh 
như FPT , chúng tôi nhận thấy xu hướng tối ưu chi phí tốt hơn nhờ khả năng sử dụng 
các mô hình AI đ ến từ đa dạng nhà cung c ấp; với chi phí trên hi ệu năng tính toán 
được cải thi ện. Trong k ịch b ản cơ s ở, chúng tôi d ự báo biên l ợi nhuận trước thu ế 
mảng CNTT nước ngoài sẽ ổn định ở mức 15.4% nhờ duy trì chi ến lược quản trị chi 
phí hiệu quả qua đó giúp LNTT 2026/2027 đạt 6,198/7,087 tỷ VND (13.3%/14.3% YoY).

Dựa trên định giá FCFF và P/E, triển vọng kinh doanh cũng như xem xét các rủi ro có 
thể phát sinh, chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu FPT . Giá mục tiêu là 86,000 
VND/cổ phiếu.

Các mảng kinh doanh duy trì ổn định 
giúp lợi nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ 
tăng trưởng nhẹ

Sản lượng điện của REE tăng trưởng 
nhẹ nhờ mức tăng của mảng điện gió 
sau khi dự án Duyên Hải V1-4 vận 
hành

Mảng M&E ghi nhận giá trị ký mới cao 
trong 1H.2026, t ạo kỳ vọng hồi phục 
mạnh mẽ doanh thu

Mảng cho thuê văn phòng đứng trước 
cơ hội lớn đóng góp vào tăng trư ởng 
nhờ các điều kiện thuận lợi

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
58,100 VNĐ/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu Quý 2/2026 giảm nhẹ, đạt 2,432 tỷ VND (-3% YoY/-2% QoQ), trong khi l ợi 
nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ đạt 632 tỷ VND (+1% YoY/-11.5% QoQ), do doanh thu của 
mảng bất động sản suy giảm. Qua đó, REE hoàn thành lần lượt 40%/47.8% kế hoạch 
doanh thu và LNST công ty mẹ 2026.

Trong Q2.2026, tổng sản lượng phát đi ện của REE tăng nh ẹ, đạt 2.8 tỷ kWh (+2.3% 
YoY) với đóng góp tăng trưởng rõ nét đến từ mảng điện gió khi dự án Duyên Hải V1-4 
bắt đầu vận hành; tuy nhiên  sản lượng thủy điện suy gi ảm do tác đ ộng tiêu c ực từ 
pha thời tiết El Nino, đạt 1.1 tỷ kWh.

Với năng lực chuyên môn được nâng cao, mảng M&E ghi nhận những dấu hiệu phục 
hồi tích cực trong giá trị hợp đồng ký mới trong 1H.2026, 5,865 tỷ VND (cao hơn 53.9% 
so với giá trị ký mới cả năm 2025). KBSV dự phóng doanh thu c ủa mảng M&E sẽ đạt 
4,499/5,190 tỷ VND (+28%/15% YoY) trong 2026/2027.

Hội tụ bởi 3 yếu tố: i) Định hướng phát triển kinh tế Hồ Chí Minh trở thành trung tâm 
tài chính quốc tế; ii) Hạ tầng giao thông ngày một được hoàn thiện và iii) Chi phí thuê 
cạnh tranh so với chất lượng dịch vụ của E. Town 6, KBSV dự phóng doanh thu mảng 
cho thuê văn phòng trong 2026/2027 tăng trưởng +14%/6.2% YoY.

Dựa trên đ ịnh giá SOTP , triển vọng kinh doanh cũng như xem xét các r ủi ro có th ể 
phát sinh, chúng tôi khuy ến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu REE. Giá m ục tiêu là 58,100 
VND/cổ phiếu, cao hơn 28.3% so với mức giá đóng cửa tại ngày 15/09/2026.

Doanh thu 2Q.26 bứt phá nhờ khoản 
thu nhập bất thường của nhiệt điện 
Cà Mau và Vũng Áng

Nhiệt đi ện than duy trì công su ất ở 
mức cao nh ờ nguồn cung nhiên li ệu 
ổn định trong 2H26

Điện khí Đông Nam Bộ (NT1&2 và 
NT3&4) có triển vọng hoạt động 
thuận lợi

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
18,100 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Trong Quý 2/2026, POW ghi nhận Doanh thu và LNST cổ đông công ty mẹ đạt lần lượt 
20,308 tỷ VND (116.1% YoY) và 3,519 tỷ VND (484.6% YoY) chủ yếu nhờ các khoản thu 
nhập từ chênh lệch tiền khí của nhiệt điện Cà Mau và tỷ giá của nhiệt điện Vũng Áng. 
Với kết qu ả trên, POW hoàn thành l ần lư ợt 65%/420% kế hoạch doanh thu và l ợi 
nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ.

Chúng tôi cho r ằng nhà máy nhi ệt than Vũng Áng trong 2026 s ẽ được duy trì công 
suất huy động ở mức cao nhờ: i) Xác suất diễn ra El Nino tăng cao và dự kiến kéo dài 
đến đầu 2027, ii) Nguồn cung than trong nước được bảo đảm từ các đối tác (TKV, TCT 
Đông B ắc) và d ự trữ than d ồi dào c ủa doanh nghi ệp ghi nh ận ở mức cao trong 
Q2/2026.

Dự án đường dây 500kV Mạch 3 đi vào vận hành giúp giảm áp lực cạnh tranh của các 
nhà máy điện tại khu vực phía Nam, qua đó kỳ vọng doanh thu của NT1&2 trong năm 
2026 đạt 11,104 tỷ VND (5.3% YoY). Đối với dự án điện NT3&4, khoản thu nhập chênh 
lệch sản lượng theo giá công suất do sản lượng điện thực tế thấp hơn so với tỷ lệ Qc 
65% của nhà máy được duy trì sang 2H26 và 2027 sẽ giúp cho dự án ghi nhận doanh 
thu lần lượt 17,848/17,621 tỷ VND doanh thu trong 2026/2027.

Dựa trên định giá SOTP và EV/EBITDA, tri ển vọng kinh doanh cũng như xem xét các 
rủi ro có th ể phát sinh, chúng tôi khuy ến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu POW. Giá m ục 
tiêu là 18,100 VND/cổ phiếu.

CTCP Dịch vụ Hàng không Taseco (AST) 
Tiếp tục mở rộng điểm kinh doanh

Chuyên viên cao cấp Nguyễn Ngọc Anh 
(+84) 24-7303-5333 anhntn@kbsec.com.vn

KQKD tăng trư ởng tích c ực trong 
2Q2026

Tăng trư ởng khách qua c ảng hàng 
không thúc đ ẩy doanh thu t ại các 
điểm kinh doanh hiện hữu

Mở rộng mạng lưới tạo động lực tăng 
trưởng doanh thu mới

Cơ c ấu kinh doanh và hi ệu qu ả vận 
hành hỗ trợ cải thiện biên lợi nhuận

Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA, giá mục 
tiêu 76,500 VND/cổ phiếu

2Q2026, AST ghi nh ận doanh thu và LNST đ ạt 479/92 t ỷ VND (+22%/19% yoy), với 
động lực chủ yếu đến từ (1) đóng góp từ 19 điểm kinh doanh mở mới tại T3 Tân Sơn 
Nhất và (2) tăng trưởng tích cực của các mảng hàng miễn thuế, nhà hàng - fastfood 
và phòng ch ờ thương gia. Lũy k ế 6T2026, doanh thu và LNST đ ạt 1,035/221 t ỷ VND 
(+33%/46% yoy), lần lượt hoàn thành 48%/49% kế hoạch năm.

Chúng tôi dự phóng lượng khách quốc tế/nội địa tại các thị trường AST khai thác tăng 
bình quân 11%/5% mỗi năm đến 2030. Lưu lượng và tỷ trọng khách quốc tế gia tăng 
được kỳ vọng thúc đẩy số giao dịch và doanh thu bình quân trên mỗi khách, đặc biệt 
tại mảng miễn thuế, bán lẻ và phòng chờ.

Chúng tôi dự phóng số điểm kinh doanh AST tăng từ 145 điểm cuối năm 2026 lên 182 
điểm năm 2030. Sau đóng góp t ừ 19 điểm tại T3 Tân Sơn Nh ất và 6 điểm mới tại T2 
Nội Bài từ 2025 - 2026, các dự án Long Thành, Phú Qu ốc và Gia Bình ti ếp tục mở ra 
dư địa mở rộng mạng lưới trong trung và dài hạn cho doanh nghiệp.

Việc mở rộng các hoạt động mang lại lợi nhuận cao, bao gồm hàng miễn thuế, phòng 
chờ thương gia và tối ưu hoạt động tại các điểm kinh doanh đ ược kỳ vọng tác động 
tích c ực lên biên l ợi nhu ận của AST . Chúng tôi dự phóng biên l ợi nhu ận g ộp đ ạt 
64.7%/65.5% trong năm 2026/2027, hỗ trợ LNST công ty mẹ tăng lần lượt 48%/17% 
yoy.

Chúng tôi khuy ến nghị MUA đối với AST , giá mục tiêu 76 ,500 VND/cổ phiếu, tương 
đương tiềm năng tăng giá 20% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 21/09/2026.

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
59,000 VNĐ/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu 2Q2026 đạt 1,222 tỷ (-8% YoY/18.1% QoQ) và LNST công ty mẹ đạt 325 tỷ 
VND (7% YoY/55% QoQ), ghi nhận tăng trưởng của tất cả các mảng. Mảng cung cấp 
nước tăng trưởng ổn định, tiếp tục là trụ cột chính của BWE, đóng góp doanh thu 785 
tỷ VND (+12% YoY/12% QoQ) nhờ nhà máy nước Long An v ận hành từ Q3/2025 giúp 
sản lượng nước tăng 28% YoY.

Cường độ và thời gian kéo dài c ủa các đ ợt nắng nóng trong 2026/2027 là cơ s ở để 
chúng tôi đi ều chỉnh tăng tăng trư ởng tiêu th ụ nước của nhóm khách hàng dân cư 
tại Bình Dương và Bình Phư ớc trong năm 2026 - 2027 lên mức 7.5/6.5% YoY. Trái lại, 
triển vọng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước nhóm sản xuất ảm đạm do tình hình thuế quan 
có diễn biến bất lợi trong ngắn hạn cho các ngành xu ất khẩu truyền thống của Việt 
Nam.

UBND TP .HCM công bố đơn giá thu gom, vận chuyển và xử lý rác thải mới, tăng mạnh 
từ 50-150% nhằm phản ánh đầy đủ và chính xác hơn các chi phí phát sinh trong thực 
tế. Chúng tôi cho rằng điều này sẽ tác động tích cực với BWE khi các dự án của doanh 
nghiệp ở mảng này s ẽ vận hành hi ệu quả hơn. Dự báo doanh thu và l ợi nhuận gộp 
của mảng xử lý rác thải lần lượt đạt 1,069/1,234 và 165.7/180.7 tỷ VND lần lượt trong 
2026/2027, tương ứng mức tăng 34%/15% và 27%/9% YoY.

cửa ngày 22/09/2026.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX điều chỉnh trở lại trong tuần qua sau khi gặp thử thách tại vùng cản quanh 1850, 
hình thành cây nến tuần giảm điểm nhưng với phần bóng dưới khá dài cho thấy sự tham 
gia tích cực của lực cầu bắt đáy tại vùng hỗ trợ gần. Trên khung ngày, nhịp giảm mạnh giữa 
tuần đã khiến chỉ số đánh mất đáy ngắn hạn tuần trước và chớm cắt xuống dưới SMA200, 
trong khi phiên hồi phục cuối tuần chưa cho thấy sự cải thiện rõ rệt của lực cầu. Mặc dù 
diễn biến này cho thấy áp lực điều chỉnh vẫn có thể còn tiếp diễn, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng 
tăng ngắn hạn chưa bị phá vỡ, VNINDEX được kỳ vọng sẽ nhận được lực đỡ tại vùng hỗ trợ 
gần quanh 1750, tương ứng vùng hợp lưu quanh SMA50, và có cơ hội hồi phục trở lại.

## Document 206: Cliff Notes_ searching for signal (2026-10-17)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ searching for signal (2026-10-17).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-17
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: searching for signal
By Sian Fenner, Ryan Wells & Illiana Jain
13:33 October 02 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia, this week’s main event was the RBA Monetary Policy Board’s (MPB) decision to raise the
cash rate by 25bps to 4.60%. This outcome was universally expected given Governor Bullock had
already communicated that upside risks to inflation had materialised – the conditionality established by

the MPB back in August for considering another rate hike.

### Page 2

Governor Bullock made explicit note of three upside risks. The Middle East conflict was emphasised as
the risk that had “materialised”, given the re-escalation and renewed surge in crude oil and refined
product prices over recent months. The RBA’s liaison warns this could lead to further pass-through of
costs and more persistent inflation pressures. On top of this, domestic capacity pressures and the data
centre/AI investment boom were also noted as upside risks that the MPB remain alert to.

In a video update mid-week, Chief Economist Luci Ellis explains why the bar for a follow-up rate hike in
November is low. We now expect a 25bp increase in November, provided there is no sudden resolution
of the Middle East conflict beforehand, or some other event that significantly lowers the outlook for
energy-related costs.
Subsequently, the August CPI revealed that underlying (trimmed mean) inflation rose 0.2% (3.4%yr),
which was slightly below the market’s expectation but spot on Westpac’s forecast. As usual, the

monthly detail was mixed, but the key drivers of underlying inflation remain centred on housing-related
components, particularly rents and new dwelling purchases. Headline inflation remained volatile, with
the unwind of the fuel excise cut causing another spike in fuel prices. We have maintained our forecast
for a 0.9% lift in trimmed mean inflation in Q3, and with upside risks building for Q4, a follow-up rate
hike in November appears most likely.

Regarding the current growth pulse, household spending surprised with a flat reading in August.
Services spend fell back following recent strength that was driven by major sporting events, but this
was offset by an increase in goods spend, particularly in fuel and electric vehicles. Together with our
latest card data for early September, the consumer spending pulse is shading a touch softer. However,
another solid outcome for business investment could once again be on the cards for Q3. August’s

goods trade data showcased another significant spike in data centre-related equipment imports, up
$4.3bn, similar in scale to Q1. While clearly import-driven, our modelling suggests that the local
economic impact of such investment is likely to be around half of the total investment.
Before moving offshore, a final note on housing. Cotality’s latest update revealed that dwelling prices
fell another 1.1% nationally in September. While Sydney and Melbourne have led the correction to

date, the fact that the other capitals are also reporting price declines clearly indicates that the
correction is deepening and broadening, as higher interest rates collide with the tax policy changes
announced in May’s budget. Sentiment effects indeed remain at play, as evinced by the continued pull-
back in investor credit growth. Westpac anticipates a peak-to-trough decline of around 7%, with tight
on-market supply limiting the extent of the correction. For more, see our latest Housing Pulse.

Offshore, there was plenty of data to dissect.
In the US, inflation eased in August despite ongoing strength in household demand and higher energy
prices. Headline PCE inflation rose 0.3%mth and 3.4%yr, while core PCE increased 0.2%mth and
3.0%yr. Encouragingly for the FOMC, the share of PCE components rising by more than 3% over the
past year, a measure frequently cited by Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh, declined to 45% from 49%,
suggesting some easing in broad-based inflation pressures. At the same time, household spending

remained robust, increasing 0.9%mth, equivalent to around 0.6%mth in real terms. That said, personal
income growth was soft, rising 0.2% in the month, signalling households opted to draw down savings.

### Page 3

The ISM manufacturing survey was stronger than the headline suggested. While the index edged down
0.1pt to 54.5, manufacturing has now expanded for nine consecutive months. There were some signs
of capacity constraints emerging, with production softened in the month but backlogs up 4.6pts to 56.4,
suggesting that firms struggled to keep pace with demand. New orders rose 1.6pts to 55.3, primarily
due to domestic demand as new export orders eased, backlogs increased. Firms responded by lifting

employment, 1.5pts to 52.7. Against this backdrop manufacturers saw a renewed acceleration in cost
pressures, with the prices paid index jumping 6.8pts to 77.9. Firms cited higher steel and aluminium
prices, elevated energy costs, rising freight charges and ongoing semiconductor shortages, with every
major industry reporting higher input costs during the month.
The past week’s data will have done nothing to bridge the clear divide across the FOMC, as evident in

this week’s busy Fedspeak. Some participants argued for further rate hikes, citing strong inflation,
diminished supply capacity and policy that is not yet restrictive. Others counselled patience, pointing to
progress on inflation and that September’s hike buys time to assess upcoming data. The one thing
both agree on is that inflation will be the primary determinant of the timing and extent of further rate
hikes. Our view remains that one further hike is likely this cycle to bring policy closer to neutral.

In China, policymakers announced another round of targeted support measures aimed at stabilising
growth and supporting the struggling property sector. The PBOC lowered rates on its Pledged
Supplementary Lending facility by 25bps to 1.5% and expanded quotas for targeted lending programs.
Authorities also introduced subsidies for eligible first-home buyers. The subsidy applies to homes with
a floor area of 120m² or less and a value below CNY1.5mn, with support capped at 1ppt of mortgage

costs, up to CNY10,000 and for a maximum term of five years. The measures complement reforms
announced in August to improve developer financing conditions and support housing completion.
However, weak credit demand and confidence remains a significant constraint, suggesting policy
transmission is still limited.
The September PMI data pointed to a modest improvement in activity, although the details were less
convincing. Manufacturing returned to expansionary territory, rising 0.9pts to 50.1, supported by

stronger production and new orders. Much of the improvement, however, reflected a rebound in prices,
with the raw materials price index rising 7.6pts and producer prices increasing 6.2pts. Labour market
conditions remained weak, with the employment index falling a further 0.6pts and remaining below 50
for almost four years. Conditions in the non-manufacturing sector also improved, with the services PMI
lifting from 49.0 to 50.2, driven largely by stronger construction activity. Input and output prices moved

higher, selling prices returned back above 50, export orders improved to 48.5 and employment
increased 0.5pts, although at 45.9 it remains firmly in contractionary territory. Overall, the surveys
suggest activity is stabilising and policy support is gaining some traction, but weak labour demand,
subdued credit growth and soft domestic demand indicate the recovery remains fragile.
In Japan, the September Tankan survey delivered another constructive signal on the economy and

reinforced the case for further policy normalisation. Business conditions improved by 2pts for large
manufacturers and 5pts more broadly, while profitability indicators strengthened and capital
expenditure intentions remained elevated. Large manufacturers expect fixed investment to increase by
11.6% in FY2026, while large non-manufacturers expect investment growth of 11.2%, pointing to

### Page 4

Related articles
continued confidence in the medium-term outlook.
Capacity indicators improved further across both manufacturing and non-manufacturing sectors, with
measures of excess production capacity becoming less negative and indicating that demand remains
sufficiently strong to keep pressure on existing capacity. Labour shortages also intensified, with
employment conditions becoming more negative across most industries and firm sizes, pointing to a

progressively tighter labour market. Inflation expectations remained anchored around 2%, providing
further evidence that Japan is moving towards a more sustainable inflation environment. Overall, the
survey was consistent with an economy operating close to capacity and supports the view that a
December rate hike remains a realistic possibility.

## Document 212: CoreWeave违约概率超六成！黄仁勋“灭火”：融资成本在下降，无需英伟达兜底

Source PDF: all report pdf/CoreWeave违约概率超六成！黄仁勋“灭火”：融资成本在下降，无需英伟达兜底.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

⻩仁勋讲话后，早盘曾跌 9.5%CoreWeave 股价转涨。该司五年期 CDS 周四盘中升破 1190
基点，反映债务违约保护成本的指标创历史新⾼，部分公司债收益率升⾄ 15% ⾄ 16% 以
上。甲⻣⽂、亚⻢逊、⾕歌⺟公司、 Meta 的五年期 CDS 利差均处历史⾼位。德银分析师认
为，随着 AI 资本开⽀周期越来越多地由债务、⽽⾮⾃由现⾦流⽀撑，投资者必须更重视信
⽤市场的价格信号，整体⽽⾔， AI 相关融资成本尚不算⾼，但已有些令⼈担忧的迹象。
摘要：
CoreWeave 违约⻛险飙升⾄历史新⾼、信⽤市场隐含的未来五年违约概率超过六成之际，英伟
达 CEO ⻩仁勋出⾯ “ 灭⽕ ” ，称 CoreWeave 财务实⼒持续改善、融资成本正在下降，英伟达没有
必要为其兜底。
美东时间 10 ⽉ 15 ⽇周四，反映公司债务违约保护成本的指标 CoreWeave 五年期信⽤违约互换
（ CDS ）利差⼀度上升 74.5 个基点，达到 1194.5 个基点，创盘中历史新⾼。与此同时，该公司

部分债券收益率升⾄ 15% ⾄ 16% 以上，反映投资者对其偿债能⼒的担忧急剧升温。
⻩仁勋同⽇在瑞典出席活动期间接受媒体采访时表⽰： “CoreWeave 的实⼒与⽇俱增。 ” 他称，⾃
⼰对该公司持乐观态度，并指出，该司的 “ 资本成本实际上正在下降 ” 。对于市场关注的英伟达是
否需要为 CoreWeave 提供财务兜底，⻩仁勋明确表⽰，他并不认为有此必要。
周四， CoreWeave （ CRWV ）股价⼤幅低开 6.6% ，早盘跌幅⼀度扩⼤⾄ 9.5% ，⻩仁勋讲话后，
加速收窄跌幅，午盘转涨后持续上⾏，尾盘⽇内涨幅曾略超 6% ，较早盘刷新的⽇低反弹约
17% 。到收盘，股价涨逾 5.9% ，扭转三⽇连跌，本周内的累计跌幅收窄到 15% 左右。
更值得警惕的是，信⽤市场的紧张情绪在蔓延，显⽰投资者正重新评估 AI 基础设施投资背后的债
务⻛险。媒体指出，甲⻣⽂五年期 CDS 利差周四已接近 300 个基点，本周四⽇升约 31 个基点，和
亚⻢逊、⾕歌⺟公司 Alphabet 、 Meta 的相关利差均处于历史⾼位；软银五年期 CDS 利差⼀⽇扩

⼤ 24 个基点；英伟达、意法半导体和 SpaceX 的 CDS 利差也明显⾛⾼。

### Page 2

五年期 CDS 突破 1190 基点， CoreWeave 债券遭抛售
CoreWeave 正遭遇股债双杀。
ICE Data Services 数据显⽰，周四 CoreWeave 五年期 CDS 利差盘中升⾄ 1194.5 个基点，创有记
录以来新⾼，相关定价对应的未来五年累计违约概率超过 60% 。 LSEG 及 S&P Global Market
Intelligence 数据显⽰， CoreWeave 的 CDS 利差周四盘中突破 1100 个基点，对应的五年期违约⻛
险约为 64% ，⾼于⼀年前约 30% 的⽔平。
CDS 是⽤于对冲债务违约⻛险的信⽤衍⽣品，其利差通常随市场对发⾏⼈偿债能⼒的担忧加剧
⽽上升。不过， CDS 隐含的违约概率取决于定价模型及相关假设，并不意味着公司未来五年实
际违约概率已被精确测定。
债券市场的表现同样严峻。彭博援引⾏情数据称，周四盘中， CoreWeave 于 2031 年到期、票⾯
利率为 9% 的债券价格下跌约 3.3 美分，跌⾄每美元⾯值 79.9 美分，收益率升⾄ 15.6% ；另⼀只票

⾯利率为 9.75% 、同样于 2031 年到期的债券下跌约 2.6 美分，跌⾄ 78.65 美分，收益率升⾄
16.2% 。
路透报道称， CoreWeave 于 2032 年到期的债券在 Tradeweb 平台上的交易价格约为每美元⾯值
78 美分，收益率超过 16% 。不同债券的到期⽇、票⾯利率和交易价格有所不同，但都显⽰出投
资者正在要求更⾼的⻛险补偿。
债券价格⼤幅低于⾯值、收益率飙升，意味着 CoreWeave 未来通过债券市场融资或再融资可能
⾯临更⾼成本。
CoreWeave 联合创始⼈ Brannin McBee ⽇前接受《⾦融时报》采访时表⽰，公司希望获得投资
级信⽤评级，从⽽打开更⼴泛的融资渠道，甚⾄能够借⼊ “ ⽆限 ” 规模的资⾦池。这⼀⽬标与当下
⾼企的债券收益率形成鲜明反差：在投资者对偿债能⼒的担忧尚未消退之际，公司能否以更低
成本持续获得融资，仍有待市场检验。
甲⻣⽂、软银等 AI 相关企业 CDS ⾛⾼，信⽤⻛险向⾏业扩散
CoreWeave 并⾮唯⼀遭遇信⽤市场压⼒的 AI 相关企业。

### Page 3

周四盘中，甲⻣⽂五年期 CDS 利差报 288 个基点，与前⼀交易⽇持平，但较⼀周前的 260 个基点
扩⼤ 28 个基点；软银五年期 CDS 利差单⽇扩⼤ 24 个基点⾄ 424 个基点。与此同时，英伟达、意
法半导体和 SpaceX 的 CDS 利差也明显⾛⾼。
据 LSEG 及 S&P Global Market Intelligence 数据，亚⻢逊、 Alphabet 和 Meta 的 CDS 利差也升⾄
历史⾼位，不过整体上升速度慢于甲⻣⽂和 CoreWeave 。这些科技巨头的信⽤保险成本同样受
到 AI 投资融资⻛险重估的影响。
CDS 利差衡量投资者为防范债务违约⻛险所需⽀付的成本。利差⾛阔意味着市场要求更⾼的⻛
险补偿，但不等于企业即将违约，也不能将不同公司的 CDS 利差直接视为可相互⽐较的违约概
率。
多家 AI 相关企业的信⽤保险成本上升，反映出投资者正在重新审视 AI 基础设施投资的融资⽅式。
甲⻣⽂等企业的⼤规模借款需求受到关注， CoreWeave 则因负债沉重、仍未实现盈利且⾼度依

赖外部融资，成为市场压⼒最集中的案例。
路透社指出，美国通胀数据⾼于预期、市场对未来利率⾛向的担忧升温，加剧了投资者对 AI 巨额
资本开⽀回报周期的疑虑。随着⾏业越来越多地依赖债务⽽⾮⾃由现⾦流为 AI 建设融资，市场关
注点正从算⼒需求增⻓本⾝，转向相关企业能否及时创造⾜够利润和现⾦流，以覆盖不断增加
的利息⽀出。
约 500 亿美元债务压顶， CoreWeave 的扩张模式遭拷问
CoreWeave ⾯临的核⼼问题，是资本开⽀增⻓与现⾦流、融资能⼒之间的平衡。
媒体指出，这家 AI 云计算公司背负近 500 亿美元债务，且仍处于亏损状态。公司依靠⼤规模采购
英伟达 GPU 、建设数据中⼼并扩充算⼒容量来满⾜客⼾需求，⽽这类投资通常需要在收⼊逐步
兑现之前投⼊⼤量资⾦。
CoreWeave 已⼤举增加资本开⽀，以抢占快速增⻓的 AI 算⼒市场。在需求强劲的阶段，举债扩
张能够帮助企业提前建设基础设施，并利⽤⻓期客⼾合同⽀撑投资计划。但数据中⼼建设周期
⻓、设备投⼊⼤，⼀旦融资成本上升、项⽬延期或客⼾需求增⻓不及预期，企业就可能⾯临资

⾦缺⼝和偿债压⼒。
CoreWeave 的问题因此不只是债务规模⾼，⽽是市场正在质疑其未来现⾦流能否覆盖资本开
⽀、利息⽀出及其他经营成本。债券收益率上升⼜会进⼀步抬⾼新增融资的⻔槛，使这⼀问题
更加突出。
CoreWeave 的管理层则强调订单储备充⾜，并认为市场对其财务状况存在误判。但订单储备并
不等同于已经收到的现⾦：⻓期合同能够提供⼀定的收⼊可⻅性，却不能⾃动消除建设成本超
⽀、交付延迟、融资条件变化及客⼾集中度等⻛险。投资者需要判断的是，这些合同最终能否
转化为⾜够的现⾦流和可持续利润。
英伟达为何出⾯ “ 灭⽕ ” ，供应商融资与客⼾需求交织
⻩仁勋公开为 CoreWeave 释放信⼼，背后是英伟达与这家 AI 云服务商之间紧密的商业和资本联
系。
英伟达持有 CoreWeave 约 10% 的股份，是其第⼆⼤股东。同时， CoreWeave 也是英伟达 GPU 的
重要采购⽅，其商业模式依赖采购英伟达芯⽚、建设数据中⼼，再将算⼒租赁给 AI 开发商和⼤型
科技企业。

### Page 4

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
如果 CoreWeave 融资受阻，后续数据中⼼建设、 GPU 采购及算⼒交付都可能受到影响。即便现
有订单不会⽴即取消，融资成本上升也可能改变项⽬建设节奏和资本回报预期，进⽽影响市场
对英伟达 AI 需求增⻓的判断。
更敏感的问题在于，供应商投资客⼾、客⼾采购供应商产品的安排，容易引发市场对 “ 循环融资 ”
的担忧。
这类交易本⾝并不意味着存在不当⾏为，但当企业依赖借款购买供应商的产品，⽽供应商⼜持
有企业股权时，投资者会追问：产业链的增⻓究竟有多少来⾃最终客⼾的真实需求，⼜有多少
依赖融资推动的持续扩张？
⻩仁勋强调 CoreWeave 的融资成本正在下降，并明确表⽰⽆需英伟达兜底，既是在维护重要合
作伙伴的市场信⼼，也是在避免市场将双⽅的商业联系直接解读为英伟达必须承担 CoreWeave

债务⻛险。
不过，这番表态并不能替代对 CoreWeave 资产负债表的审视。英伟达持有股权并不等于对其债
务提供担保，⻩仁勋的乐观判断也⽆法直接解决公司的融资需求、利息负担和现⾦流问题。
AI 投资回报能否覆盖巨额资本开⽀，成为市场核⼼问题
CoreWeave 的信⽤⻛波正在成为 AI 基础设施投资的⼀次压⼒测试。
在⽣成式 AI 需求快速增⻓的背景下，科技企业和算⼒运营商⼤举建设数据中⼼，试图抢占未来市
场。但基础设施投⼊往往先于收⼊兑现，尤其是依赖外部融资的新型云服务商，需要不断借款
购买 GPU 、建设机房并⽀付能源和运营成本。
当利率预期上升、债券投资者要求更⾼⻛险补偿时，这套模式就会⾯临更严格的检验。融资成
本增加不仅会压缩利润，还可能迫使企业放慢扩建速度；如果扩建放缓，⼜可能影响未来收⼊
增⻓和既有投资的回报。
德意志银⾏策略师  Jim Reid 指出，这并⾮⾸次因 AI 的担忧⽽经历显著的市场动荡，但随着 AI 资
本开⽀周期越来越多地由债务、⽽⾮⾃由现⾦流⽀撑，投资者必须更加重视信⽤市场的价格信

号。 “ 整体⽽⾔， AI 相关的融资成本尚不算⾼，但在⼀些边缘领域已出现令⼈担忧的迹象。 ”
这⼀判断凸显了⾏业⾯临的关键⽭盾：算⼒需求增⻓，并不⾃动等于算⼒运营商能够获得⾜够
利润；订单增⻓，也不必然意味着债务能够得到轻松偿还。
对 CoreWeave ⽽⾔，真正的考验是能否控制债务增⻓、持续获得融资，并将庞⼤的订单储备转
化为可持续的经营现⾦流。对英伟达及整个 AI 产业链⽽⾔，市场关注点则正在从 “ 还需要多少算
⼒ ” ，转向 “ 谁来为算⼒建设买单，以及这些投资最终能否产⽣⾜够回报 ” 。
⻩仁勋试图安抚市场，但 CoreWeave 债券收益率⾼企、信⽤保险费率创纪录，以及甲⻣⽂、软
银等企业 CDS 利差⾛⾼，说明投资者对 AI 融资模式的审视正在深⼊。未来， AI 产业链的估值不
仅取决于算⼒需求能否持续增⻓，也将越来越取决于企业能否以可承受的融资成本，将这场资
本开⽀竞赛转化为真实、可持续的现⾦流。

Energy shock: Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022
10/2/2026 2
Will the same causes produce the same effects? In other words, will the war in Iran and the resulting
surge in oil and gas prices lead to an inflationary shock comparable to that seen in 2022? Will their
negative effects on growth be the same as those of the war in Ukraine and the subsequent energy
shock?
We have selected a set of indicators to track the impact of this new energy shock — caused by the war

in the Middle East — on activity and prices in the Eurozone, the United States, oil and gas markets and
emerging countries, and to see how much the current situation resembles that of 2022 at the outbreak
of the conflict in Ukraine.
This dashboard features charts and comments that will be updated on a monthly basis for as long as
necessary.
Overall, based on data available through September 2026, economic activity continues to show
resilience. The high levels of business climate surveys illustrate this well. Household confidence,

however, is more vulnerable and sensitive to energy price pressures. Financing conditions in emerging
economies also continue to hold up well against the shock (much better than in 2022) as well as
against the tensions in DM bond markets, which intensified this summer, partly in the wake of the
reinforcing upward pressure on energy prices. Higher energy prices are also fueling inflationary
pressures and pushing inflation even higher. In particular, Eurozone inflation rose by 0.6 percentage

points to 3.8% year-on-year (September flash estimate), reaching its highest level in three years.

to a 3-year high

SPECIAL EDITION | HOW BAS IS THE RISE

TRANSCRIPTION

0:00:10 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Everyone, welcome. As we head into autumn 2026, interest rates are reaching levels never seen for many, many years. 
Why is this happening? Is it sustainable? And what are the implications for the economies of different countries? All these 
questions will be discussed over the next few minutes with the economists of BNP Paribas. Let us begin with Isabelle 
Mateos y Lago, chief economist of the group. Hello, Isabelle.  
0:00:38 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Hello Emmanuel, hello everyone. 
0:00:39 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you for being with us today. Just to begin, a simple explanation. What rates are we talking about and which countries 
are affected? 
0:00:48 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Yes, it is very important to clarify this. So, we are talking about long-term interest rates, rates at 10 years or longer 
maturities, 30 years, etc. The rates at which sovereign states borrow on the market. And this is primarily something that is 
happening in advanced countries, so United States, United King dom, Japan, and then the Eurozone countries. And indeed, 
as you said, we are seeing since the beginning of the summer, interest rates reach levels we had not seen for about 20 
years. But it is important to keep in mind that up until that point, up until the mid -2000s, the level of interest rates that 
we're seeing now were really quite common, quite normal, so one shouldn't overdramatize.  
0:01:35 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Why do we find ourselves in this situation and how did we get to this point?  
0:01:41 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Yes, so the direct cause is that investors are demanding higher interest rates in order to  lend their capital to governments. 
But why is this? Well, there's essentially three reasons for this, and not all of them are bad, and they're not mutually 
exclusive either. The top reason to my mind is that there's just greater competition for capital be tween sovereign states 
and the corporate sector, who are all facing simultaneously additional investment needs, in defense, in artificial 
intelligence, in energy transition, in resilience. And so,  you just have greater competition for capital and that pushes up the 
price of capital, which is the interest rate. 
Reason number two is because markets have revised upwards their expectations of how high central banks are going to be 
taking their interest rates. 
And this mechanically translates into higher rates at the long end. And then there's a third reason, which doesn't apply 
necessarily in every case, but which is essentially higher sovereign risk, a kind of a risk premium, in cases where there are  
concerns arising about the sustainability of these public debts.

### Page 2

2

0:02:59 - Emmanuel Laborde 
What are the implications for global economies? 
0:03:03 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
So that's exactly what we'll be discussing in the next segments. But basically, the two key issues are, one, an adverse 
impact on public finances. It's higher interest rates making it harder to balance the books in terms of public accounts. And 
then secondly, these higher rates tend to slow down economic growth. Of course, by how much and how serious it will be, 
depends on how high interest rates go and how long they stay there. But that's for the next segments.  
0:03:38 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Yes. Thank you very much, Isabelle. Having said the scene now, let's move on to the first segment with the following 
question. What makes debt sustainable? And let's see what Hélène Baudchon has to say.  
Hello Hélène and welcome into this special edition. 
0:03:58 - Hélène Baudchon 
Hello Emmanuel, hello everybody. 
0:04:01 - Emmanuel Laborde 
When economists talk about public debt, they often refer to the sustainability of debt. As an economist, can you explain to 
us what that means and what is the debt trajectory deemed unsustainable?  
0:04:15 - Hélène Baudchon 
To begin with, I will say that a temporary increase in debt can be seen as a normal evolution, not particularly concerning, 
for instance during an economic crisis or more recently during a health crisis. In such cases, it acts as a buffer. A problem  
arises when the debt level never comes back down. An unsustainable trajectory can be therefore defined, characterized by 
a continuous regular rise in the debt level, both in absolute terms and as a proportion of GDP, driven by high and recurring 
fiscal deficits. And sometimes interest payments can exacerbate the problem.  
0:05:00 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In very simple terms, you are talking about the snowball effect.  
0:05:03 - Hélène Baudchon 
Exactly explaining why consolidation measures are needed in order to prevent such an adverse situation from occurring 
and in order to prevent, in fact, a sudden loss of confidence from creditors.  
0:05:16 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Regarding governments, what should serve as a warning sign for them? And at what point could the situation become, let's 
say, problematic? 
0:05:27 - Hélène Baudchon 
Well, simply speaking, when interest rates rise, not so long ago, up until 2022, Eurozone countries were borrowing at very 
low rates and in some instance, even negative rates. And although debt levels were already at high level at that time, the 
low rates helped manage debt servicing. Not much fiscal effort was needed to keep the public debt ratios in check.  
0:05:55 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Of course, we all remember this because it was a very unusual situation, but now things are very different.

### Page 3

3

0:06:01 - Hélène Baudchon 
Yes, indeed, the situation today is clearly more challenging simply because bond yields are significantly higher and they 
are rising at the time of our discussion. This is for the part the result of the ECB's monetary tightening in 2022 -2023 aimed 
at addressing the inflation shock then. Then long-term interest rates have risen as a consequence and have aligned more 
closely with GDP growth rate. And in technical terms, this is what we refer to as a narrowing of the "r" - "g" spread. 
0:06:41 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Why is this spread so important? Why do you, the economists, monitor it this way?  
0:06:48 - Hélène Baudchon 
Essentially, you have the interest rate "r", which determines the debt burden, while the growth rate "g" determines the 
trajectory of fiscal revenues. And if "r" is lower than "g", and this has been the case for many years, as you can see on the  
chart on the screen, it means that interest payments are increasing at a slower pace than the revenue component of the 
equation. 
0:07:14 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And this provides some leeway in other budgetary areas, such as education, of course, defense, maybe pensions.  
0:07:22 - Hélène Baudchon 
Yes, indeed. And this is what we refer to as the primary budget. However, if and when interest rates do go up while the 
economy isn't growing that much faster, then fiscal leeway diminishes.  
0:07:35 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Crystal clear. Now, just could you provide a few last words to wrap up the sustainability segment?  
0:07:43 - Hélène Baudchon

No, it's not, Emmanuel. This is happening in the United Kingdom and in the United States. These economies, like France,

United States is not going on that path yet, but market pressure could eventually force them to do so.

0:12:26 - Thomas Humblot 
Hello, Thomas. Welcome. Hello. Thank you for having me today.  
0:12:30 - Emmanuel Laborde 
We've just seen what's behind the rise in Eurozone bond yields. This rise is partly attributed to the sovereign spread. What

0:12:42 - Thomas Humblot 
Actually, this widening of some government bond spreads is obviously not good news, but it tends paradoxically to have 
less impact than an increase in long-term interest rates that does not include the sovereign risk premium. for example, in 
France, for a very long time, it has been considered that the 10 -year OAT was the relevant benchmark for fixed loans. And 
at that time, the spread on French government bonds was relatively stable. But since 2024, that spread increased

### Page 5

5

significantly and that renders the sovereign yield a less relevant benchmark.  
And now the most relevant benchmark is the swap rate because it determines the cost of bank funding that backs new 
loans. 
0:13:56 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Since the end of 2024, we have seen a rise in long-term 10-year swap rates. What impact does this have on bank lending 
rates? 
0:14:08 - Thomas Humblot 
Fixed loans have obviously become more expensive, but to a lesser extent than if they were priced solely based on long -
term sovereign rates. And what about existing fixed-rate loans? 
There is no impact. An increase in rates for the entire maturity of a loan does not have any effect if it is a fixed -rate loan. 
Borrowers with outstanding loans are therefore protected. Obviously, new fixed loans are granted at higher rates. And this 
sharply contrasts with a floating-rates loans. 
Because with floating-rates loans, borrowers experience regular rate adjustments throughout the term of the loan. And in 
terms of bank financing? Strictly in terms of bank financing, an increase in short -term rates has an immediate and 
widespread restrictive impact on borrowers, while for long-term interest rates, the impact is more gradual, and it comes 
gradually as the outstanding amount of fixed-rate loans are progressively renewed. And the thing is that since the 
beginning of the war in Iran, we have seen a combination of the two.  
And in practical terms, the rate of new bank loans to non-financial corporations has increased about 25 basis points in the 
eurozone since the beginning of the year. 
0:16:12 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Just a few words about companies that borrow directly from the market.  
0:16:16 - Thomas Humblot

increased spreads for longer maturities.

Long-term bond yields rose significantly in advanced economies over the summer. What can we now say about emerging

Emerging bond markets held up well, which is quite unusual. The rise in long -term sovereign yields in emerging countries

Yes, this is still the case. Emerging countries still have a significant share of their debt in foreign currency. This repres ents

0:22:58 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago

squarely with governments and any attempt by these governments to instead take the easy way out by pressuring central 
banks to lower interest rates prematurely would be enormously counterproductive.

## Document 291: Q3 Job Vacancies_ another step lower (2026-10-17)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Q3 Job Vacancies_ another step lower (2026-10-17).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-17
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#labour #labour #employment
Q3 Job Vacancies: another step lower
By Neha Sharma
Economist, Westpac
13:07 October 01 2026 Share
The number of job vacancies fell by 3.0k (–0.9%qtr) between May and August. The decline
was wholly driven by the private sector, where vacancies fell 2.0%qtr. In contrast, public
sector vacancies rose 7.8%qtr.

### Page 2

Job vacancies fell by 3.0k (–0.9%qtr) between May and August, extending the previous quarter’s
decline. The May and February estimates were also revised slightly lower. As a result, vacancies are
now 1.4% below their level a year earlier. The result was at odds with partial indicators, which all
pointed to a rise in the quarter.

The decline was wholly driven by the private sector, where vacancies fell 2.0%qtr to be 1.5%yr lower.
In contrast, public sector vacancies rose 7.8%qtr, marking the strongest quarterly increase in four years
and partially reversing the sharp 7.5%qtr decline recorded in the May quarter. Public sector vacancies
were flat over the year.

Job vacancies have largely moved sideways since early 2025, though a resumption of the broader
downtrend now appears increasingly likely. Labour demand has held up reasonably well, though not
sufficiently to match growth in labour supply, resulting in a gradual easing in labour market conditions
(see here). Today’s result is also consistent with our view that this process will continue into early 2027
as the cumulative effects of high inflation and higher interest rates continue to weigh on activity and
hiring demand.

### Page 3

The vacancy-to-unemployment ratio, a key measure of labour market tightness closely monitored by
the RBA, declined from 0.48 to 0.45. The fall was the sharpest since early 2025, reflecting both a
decline in vacancies and a 6.8%qtr increase in the number of unemployed people over the quarter,
largely owing to the continued strength in labour supply. The ratio remains well above its long-run
average of around 0.2.

Given the strong growth in labour supply, the Beveridge curve provides a useful framework for
assessing recent labour market developments. By comparing vacancies and unemployment as shares
of the labour force, it controls for growth in the overall size of the labour market. Between 2022 and
2024, the labour market moved down the steep portion of the Beveridge curve, with substantial
declines in vacancies occurring alongside only modest increases in unemployment. While labour
market tightness has continued to unwind, Australia remains in this region of the curve, suggesting that
further rebalancing between labour demand and supply can occur through lower vacancies without a
material rise in unemployment.

### Page 4

All statistics mentioned below are not seasonally adjusted and hence may not add up to the seasonally
adjusted statistics referenced above.

By industry, job vacancies declined in eight of the 18 industries over the three months to August. The
largest falls were recorded in wholesale trade (–18.2%qtr), professional, scientific & technical services
(–17.4%qtr), and information media & telecommunications (–16.7%qtr). Consumer-facing sectors were
mixed, with retail trade (24.6%) and arts & recreation services (10.3%) recording strong increases,
while accommodation & food services edged 0.9% lower. Only three industries have seen vacancies
fall below their pre-pandemic levels: administrative & support services (–24.3%qtr), information media
& telecommunications (–9.1%qtr), and financial & insurance services (–7.3%qtr).

Overall market sector vacancies were flat in the quarter, meanwhile the non-market sector saw a 2.9%
drop, owing to declines in education & training (–8.2%qtr) and health care & social assistance (–
4.2%qtr). For detailed insights on industry condition, see Q2 Labour Account and the Westpac
Business Signal.

SUMMARY: AI euphoria meets macro headwinds
Tech remained a major driver of equity market performance in Q3. The
Mag 7 outperformed the broader market while the S&P 500 also

posted gains, lifted by mega-cap and tech stocks.
Still, tech’s path in Q3 was volatile. July began with a reversal of the AI
trade as investors questioned elevated valuations, the sustainability of
AI spending and the outlook for semiconductor demand. That led to a
large sell-off in chip stocks, with large falls in Q2 outperformers SK
Hynix and Samsung contributing to a sharp decline in Korea’s KOSPI.
The Philly SOX experienced its worst monthly performance since

2008.
By August, however, sentiment had improved. The S&P 500 reached
fresh record highs, even as US Treasury yields rose, supported by
stronger cloud earnings and signs of AI monetization. September
proved more challenging as higher oil prices, inflation concerns and
rising bond yields weighed on risk assets, particularly semiconductors.
The Fed also raised its policy rate by 25bp on Sep 16.
Yet, despite these macro headwinds, company guidance continued to

indicate strong AI infrastructure demand, with Micron’s stronger-than-
expected revenue guidance on Sep 30 providing further evidence of
robust demand for memory chips. Memory supply remains tight,
semiconductor capex is accelerating, and the industry’s attention has
increasingly turned to bottlenecks in optical networking and photonics,
as well as concerns around AI regulation and safety. Looking ahead,
tech is likely to remain an important driver of equity returns into Q4,

although elevated valuations and tighter financial conditions leave the
sector sensitive to earnings disappointments. Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, Bloomberg Finance LP. Updated Sep 30. Returns in local currency.
Q3 started with doubts about the AI trade but ended with renewed confidence

1. GLOBAL INDUSTRY BREAKDOWN
Tech hardware, including Lenovo, Advantech, and HP Inc, rebounded in Q3. This was partly driven by accelerating AI

server demand and the ongoing data center build-out, although continuing memory shortages raised costs for
hardware manufacturers. Cloud and server providers, like Oracle, Dell and HPE, also highlighted strong AI-related
demand. Semis and equipment were the weakest performers. Elsewhere, entertainment and telecoms posted modest
declines. Finally, in Q3 investor attention increasingly shifted to AI agents, with Meta’s Muse, Microsoft’s Copilot and

Salesforce’s Agentforce among the prominent offerings.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Bloomberg Finance LP. Median returns taken. Updated Sep 30.
Total returns for tech sectors over Q3 vs. YTD returns

### Page 5

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  Q3 2026 Tech Performance Review |  October 1, 20264
2. SEMICONDUCTORS
Chip stocks were hurt initially by a reversal of the AI trade and then by rising bond yields. The Philly SOX index fell
sharply in Q3, after its worst monthly performance in July (-21%) since 2008. Concerns about higher oil prices and
rising rates continued to weigh on the sector in Aug and Sep. SK Hynix was among the weakest performers, amid an

earnings disappointment relative to expectations and an unwinding of leveraged positions. Its Nasdaq ADRs began
trading in July. Despite these headwinds, the semis outlook remains supported by AI infrastructure demand. Within
semis, potential long-term beneficiaries include suppliers of AI infrastructure, memory including high-bandwidth
memory (HBM), advanced packaging, photonics and semiconductor equipment.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Bloomberg Finance LP. Updated Sep 30. Returns in local currency.

3.1 MAGNIFICENT 7: Microsoft’s earnings rebound
On July 30, Microsoft rose more than 15%, its strongest daily gain since 2008, following results released the previous

evening. The move added ~$450bn to its market cap, the largest one-day increase on record at the time. For context,
that daily increase is larger than the value of any company in Europe (except ASML).

3.2 MAGNIFICENT 7: Market concentration
According to our equity strategists, from end-July to mid-September, tech-exposed stocks within the S&P 500 rose
14% while the remainder declined 3%. This divergence highlights the concentration of market returns in the tech -

exposed sector. In late July, our strategists anticipated a rotation back into technology, which remains underway.

Notably, most of US tech is near recovery from their 1yr highs, unlike

4. REGIONAL BREAKDOWN
After outperforming in Q2, mainland Chinese tech stocks posted large declines this quarter, hurt by July’s sell-off.
The CSI 300 tech series led the declines. Weakness also extended across Asia more broadly, with the KOSPI, Nikkei 225
and Hang Seng Tech down in Q3, driven by a chip sell-off and concerns over further Fed hikes. European tech equities
were more resilient, with the STOXX Europe 600 Tech Index down slightly lower in Q3 but still well above its year-end

level. Within European tech, Infineon underperformed, while Orange illustrated weakness in telecoms outside the index.

Our thematic indicators also faced macro headwinds in Q3, although the fintech and blockchain ETF shown

Sector Analysis
Personal Consumption Expenditures (PCE)
• Consumer spending is holding up, and we expect real PCE to rise at around a 3.0% annualized rate
in Q3.
Consumer spending has been mixed, yet resilient in recent months. After a soft July, driven largely by

weaker durable goods purchases, retail sales rebounded strongly in August, rising at a 1.2% monthly
clip amid broadly distributed gains across retailers. The latest data have led us to lift our near-term
growth forecast, and we now look for real PCE to rise at a 3.2% annualized clip in Q3. Previous data
will also likely be revised higher on firmer control group sales data and strong services revenue in Q2,
suggesting recent consumer momentum remains intact. All told, the consumer is still in decent shape

and has capacity to keep spending. Real income growth remains modest but is beginning to improve
as inflation eases and the labor market remains broadly supportive suggesting continued consumption
even if that pace of spending may moderate into year-end.
Investment: Equipment, Intellectual Property Products and Inventories
• The AI build-out is still king in terms of the contribution to business investment, but the
broadening in capex spending beyond the tech sector is taking root.

AI-related investment continues to dominate capital allocation. There are no signs of firms scaling
back their outlays now, though at some point as AI investment matures, merely sustaining spending
at elevated levels will provide a smaller incremental boost to growth than it has in recent quarters.
Beyond AI, the tentative signs we've identified that signal a broadening in the investment backdrop
are getting a bit louder. The ISM manufacturing index has been in expansion territory for eight-

Economics | 3

### Page 4

Monthly Economics
straight months, and durable goods orders have firmed across a wider range of industries. Part of
this improvement likely reflects delayed capital spending plans finally moving forward, but inventory
rebuilding after five consecutive quarters of drawdown may be lending some support as well.

Investment: Residential
• Residential construction looks likely to taper through the rest of the year while mortgage rates and
new home inventories remain elevated.
Headwinds have intensified against residential construction. The trend in single-family construction is
downshifting, emblematic of the affordability challenges depressing buyer demand. As of this writing,

mortgage rates have risen to over 7.0% and builder confidence has declined alongside. Sales stuck in
a low gear mean that new home listings are inching higher, further discouraging new development.
Multifamily construction appears to be holding up but faces limited potential upside with vacancy
rates elevated across much of the South. We still see a potential for marginal improvement in 2027,
but higher mortgage rates dampen our outlook.
Investment: Nonresidential Structures

• Construction appears to be gaining steam, led by data centers and power. However, sluggish
commercial starts elsewhere and high interest rates are likely to keep growth relatively modest.
The AI infrastructure build out is supporting modest growth in nonresidential construction. After
moderating for a full year, private nonresidential outlays rose from April to July. Data centers and
power are propelling current momentum. Though construction has also strengthened across sports

facilities and communication infrastructure, commercial projects remain stuck in low gear. Categories
like warehousing and retail remain soft, and overall architectural billings have improved only modestly.
Looking ahead, a recent breakout in megaprojects is likely to support additional data center and
manufacturing development, while higher interest rates limit overall growth.

Net Exports & Trade
• Net exports are tracking to slice nearly two-percentage points off headline GDP growth amid
strong imports.
Imports are shaping up for another strong quarter after a surge in July and a rebound in August
retail spending suggesting continued domestic demand for product. The July import gain was again

almost entirely driven by high-tech related goods like computers & accessories, semiconductors and
telecommunications equipment. We still anticipate a bit more of a broadening out in imports to come,
perhaps as early as August as businesses replenish inventory amid continued consumer demand.
Export growth is a bit more constrained, suggesting an expansion in the trade deficit and that net
exports will slice nearly two-percentage points off headline GDP growth in Q3.

Labor Market
• We modestly raised our 2026 payroll forecast and made no material changes to our 2027 forecast.
We continue to expect the unemployment rate to end this year at 4.2%.
August's employment report reinforced that the labor market remains stable. Payroll growth has
averaged 106K over the past six months, though we expect some moderation in the months ahead.
Hiring measures from regional Fed surveys and hiring plans from small businesses have cooled from

their early summer jumps and job openings have leveled off. At the same time, initial jobless claims
remain near multi-decade lows and continue to indicate that layoffs remain contained. We expect
payroll growth to moderate to roughly 65K per month over the remainder of the year, a pace broadly
consistent with our estimate of breakeven. As hiring cools slightly and the participation rate rebounds
somewhat further, we see some scope for the unemployment rate to edge up to 4.2% by year-end.

Even with the slight increase though, labor market conditions would remain consistent with full
employment.
Inflation
• Incorporating our expectations for the upcoming BEA methodology changes and recent inflation
data, we look for the core PCE deflator to end the year at 3.2% on a Q4-over-Q4 basis, up from
3.1% in our previous forecast.
Headline inflation remains tied to the conflict in the Middle East, although underlying price pressures
continue to move in a more favorable direction. Higher oil prices are keeping some upward pressure

on energy-sensitive categories in the near term, but tariff-related goods inflation is fading and price
pressures outside a handful of technology-related categories remain limited. Meanwhile, core services
inflation remains on a moderating trend as shelter inflation continues to gradually cool and labor cost
pressures slowly ease. Taken together, inflation pressures appear less widespread than earlier this year,
Economics | 5

### Page 6

Monthly Economics
but recurring supply shocks are likely to keep the path back to 2% bumpy. We expect the core PCE
deflator to slow to 3.2% on a Q4-over-Q4 basis this year.

Fiscal Policy
• We forecast this year's federal budget deficit will total $2.1 trillion (~6.5% of GDP), and next year's
will be about the same in dollar terms and a bit smaller as a share of GDP (~6%).

The federal budget deficit continues to hover in the 6.0-6.5% of GDP range, similar to where it was
at the end of the Biden administration. The outlook for the next couple of years is one of status quo.
We doubt there will be any new, big fiscal legislation with divided government likely after the midterm
elections, but rising interest costs and steady growth in mandatory spending programs such as Social
Security and Medicare will keep deficits from shrinking much. It's not just U.S. fiscal problems putting

upward pressure on long-term rates. Other factors such as robust corporate debt issuance, policy
uncertainty and foreign factors are also playing a role. Our base case for 6.0%-6.5% of GDP budget
deficits will not help when it comes to bringing down long-term interest rates.
6 | Economics

### Page 7

U.S. Economic Outlook: September 2026 Economics
Monetary Policy & Interest Rates
• We have changed our fed funds forecast to 4.00-4.25% by year-end 2026, with no changes in
2027. Our year-end forecasts for the 10-year Treasury yield are also higher at 4.90% and 4.75%,
respectively, for this year and next.
The FOMC made clear at its September 16 meeting that stubbornly high PCE inflation this year was
the key factor behind the Committee's decision to raise rates. Given this, we think only a string of

good inflation data can bail the Fed out, and accordingly we think one more rate hike is coming this
year, with softer inflation data in 2027 giving way to a more patient FOMC. We could easily envision
one more hike beyond that or a "one and done" hike where the FOMC does not go again. Outcomes
outside that range, however, strike us as unlikely. A rising neutral rate makes meaningful rate cuts hard
to justify in the absence of an economic slowdown, while the case for a more aggressive tightening

cycle seems weak given that the labor market is not overheating and much of the excess inflation is
supply side driven. We think a very short gradual tightening cycle and an eventual reduction in energy
prices/slowdown in inflation will allow Treasury yields to drift modestly lower in 2027. But, the factors
mentioned above (e.g., large budget deficits, rising neutral rate) should prevent yields from falling in a
bigger way.

International Developments
• Global growth has proved more resilient than expected, prompting us to upgrade our 2026 global
GDP growth forecast to 2.9%. We have also modestly revised our global inflation outlook higher,
lifting our 2026 CPI forecast to 4.4% and our 2027 forecast accordingly.
• We now expect more tightening across advanced economies than in our previous monthly forecast
update, while emerging market policy paths remain mixed.

Global economic activity proved more resilient than expected in H1-2026, with early indicators
suggesting that momentum carried into Q3. While precautionary stockpiling supported some of the
earlier strength, activity has remained resilient on firmer services, continued AI-related investment and
exports (U.S., South Korea, Japan and Australia) and increased defense spending (Eurozone and Japan).
We expect inflation to remain higher for longer before easing next year and averaging 4% in 2027 as

tighter financial conditions weigh on demand and supply pressures fade. Risks remain skewed to the
upside. Oil and product inventories remain below pre-conflict levels, while limited refining capacity and
ongoing disruptions leave energy prices vulnerable to renewed pressure. El Niño and higher fertilizer
costs threaten food prices, while AI-related demand is raising semiconductor costs. Second-round
effects remain limited, but successive supply shocks increase the risk of persistent inflation into next

year.

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/2/27以來漲跌幅，中國信託商業銀行彙整，2026/9。
能源價格居高，天然氣價格漲幅更甚原油
美伊衝突爆發以來布蘭特原油與TTF天然氣期貨價格漲幅(%)
TTF天然氣期貨價格 布蘭特原油期貨價格 (%)
《歐元區經濟較預期具韌性，通膨壓力上升》
歐元區經濟在面臨中東衝突引發的能源成本飆升
下仍具韌性 。最新公布的9月歐元區綜合 PMI初值

升至53.1，超出預期 ，其中 ，製造業 PMI持平在
52.7，服務業PMI回升至53.0。個別國家中，德國
綜合PMI初值回升至 53.8，以2025年10月以來最
快速度擴張 ，AI與國防支出增加提供推動力 。然
而，能源價格居高不下 、天然氣價格漲幅更甚原
油，加大歐元區通膨壓力 ，投入成本和產出價格
均以四個月來最大幅度上漲。
從美伊雙方的態度來看 ，中東地緣僵局以及由此
帶來的能源價格壓力可能會持續一段時間 。歐元
區7月工業生產與零售銷售數據均較為疲軟 ，若能
源價格持續維持高檔 ，恐將推升企業成本並壓縮
家庭實質所得，對製造業與消費需求帶來壓力。
整體而言 ，歐元區經濟仍具韌性 ，但能源價格上
升所帶來的通膨與需求壓力 ，將是後續景氣的重
要觀察變數。

《歐洲央行9月會議如預期升息一碼，12月再次升
息可能性提高》
歐洲央行為了抑制通膨 ，2026年以來已升息二碼 ，
將存款機制利率上調至 2.5%。在歐央最新基準情
境預測中，2026、2027年歐元區GDP成長率預測
上修至0.9%、1.4%，然而，中東衝突持續、荷姆
茲海峽航運受阻及能源價格持續波動下 ，歐元區
經濟前景仍高度不確定。
通膨預估將在更長時間高於政策目標 ，基準情境
預估通膨率將在 4Q26升至3.6%高峰後，2Q27降
至2.5%。能源價格上升的影響將逐步傳導至非能
源價格 ，核心通膨預估 1Q27升至2.8%峰值後才
緩步回落，2027、2028年核心通膨率預測分別上

修0.1個百分點至 2.6%、2.3%，高於目標水準 。
不過，透過薪資傳導的二輪效應預期仍有限。
後續歐央政策將取決於能源價格衝擊的強度與持
續時間 ，以及是否進一步傳導至核心通膨 、薪資
與通膨預期。本行預期9月升息後，10月會議歐洲
央行將傾向觀望 ，然而，布蘭特原油期貨價格維
持在90美元/桶以上高檔 ，歐洲TTF天然氣期貨價
格漲幅更甚原油 ，在經濟維持韌性下 ，能源價格
維持高檔時間拉長 、核心通膨顯現能源價格的間
接傳導若增強 ，本行預估12月會議歐洲央行高機
率將再升息一碼，將存款機制利率升至2.75%。
資料來源：ECB，2026/9/10，中國信託商業銀行彙整，2026/9。

重點摘要
4
• 公債殖利率跳升對歐股評價
帶來壓力。
《公債殖利率跳升，歐股承壓》
九月份以來全球公債殖利率大幅攀升，對歐股造成
壓力。德國十年期公債殖利率9月份至9/28止跳升
32個基點至3.64%，創2009年6月以來新高。若從
2月底中東地緣衝突爆發以來起算 ，德國十年債利
率累積彈升了近100個基點。本波歐洲公債殖利率
走高來自多重因素疊加，能源價格上漲推升通膨、
市場重估歐央貨幣政策路徑，同時，財政支出令歐
洲公債供給增加與歐央持續縮表使私人部門需要吸
收更多公債供給，推升期限溢價，財政疑慮使部分
歐洲國家利差承受額外壓力，加上全球利率上升的
外溢影響，共同推升殖利率走高。
公債殖利率彈升令歐洲股市評價受到壓抑 ，9月份

以來至9/28止，STOXX歐洲600指數下跌近 2%。
類股中除了能源類股隨油價走高而漲逾 5%，銀行
股收紅外，其餘類股普跌，汽車及零件、基本資源
與零售股下跌7%以上。

## Document 494: 固本收利：公債殖利率全線走高，非投等債優勢盡顯

公債殖利率全線走高，非投等債優勢盡顯 
全球長債殖利率因財政赤字及債務問題而逐步走高，而短天期公債殖利率也受
到升息預期影響而上揚， 使公債 、 投等債表現相對壓抑 。 建議應逢利率高位布局
非投等債 ETF 或基金，因存續期間短及票面利率高而有望獲得較佳表現。

221 期

### Page 2

臺北市大安區仁愛路四段 169 號 
固本收利

⚫ 長天期公債反映赤
字問題 
⚫ 短天期公債聚焦

重摘要

■ 近期全球長天期公債殖利率攀升，主
因各國財政赤字與債務規模持續惡化。
CBO 預估美國未來十年公共債務占
GDP 比率將由 99%升至 120%，財政
疑慮成為長端利率的重要推升力量。

■ Fed 主席 Warsh 釋出偏鷹訊號，市場
重新提高升息機率，使 2 年期公債殖
利率快速上揚。短端利率漲幅大於長
端，殖利率曲線再度趨平。

■ 在利率全面走高環境下，非投等債憑
藉較短存續期間與較高票息維持相對
優勢 。 非投等債信用利差小幅收斂 ， 反
映市場仍看好全球經濟韌性，其中能
源產業表現尤為突出。

美國公債殖利率全線走升

資料來源：Bloomberg/北富銀整理, 2026/9/9

重點摘要 
公債殖利率全線走高，非投等債優勢盡顯

### Page 3

2026

02-8751-6665 
 www.fubon.com 
臺北市大安區仁愛路四段 169 號

▋ 長天期公債反映赤字問題 
近期全球長天期公債殖利率再次飆升 ， 主要
反應各國財政赤字及債務總額持續飆高等
負面因素。媒體多以「美國總債務站上 40
兆美元」 等標題發稿 ， 引發市場對美國財政
問題的擔憂。

圖一：美國公共債務佔 GDP 比率持續上升

資料來源：美國國會預算辦公室(CBO), 2026/02

以美國國會預算辦公室最新(2026 年 2 月)
估計來看，未來 10 年美國財政赤字率不僅
沒有縮小 ， 反而預期在2032 年後持續擴大
至 6%以上，而名目經濟成長率卻預期在
2028 年後長期維持在 3.8%的水準，一來
一往將使美國公共債務佔 GDP 比率持續上
升，由 99.4% (2025 年 ) 一路上揚至
120.2%(2036 年)。

▋ 短天期公債聚焦 FED 官員態度 
美國短天期公債也自 8/28 開始快速飆升，
2 年期公債殖利率由 4.23%上升至 4.40%
水準，主要反應聯準會主席 Warsh 在全球
央行年會的鷹派發言(內容概括認為通膨仍
過高、重申 2%通膨目標，並暗示若通膨未
明顯改善 ， 必要時將支持升息)， 即便後續有
過去相對鷹派的 FED 理事 Waller 出面緩和
情緒並表示 「若通膨持續降溫，他傾向在9
月的政策會議上維持利率不變」 ， 但市場仍
持續押注 9 月聯準會會議升息的預期。

圖二 ： 升息預期扭轉 ， 殖利率曲線再次趨平

資料來源：Bloomberg/北富銀整理, 2026/9/9

即便美國公債殖利率走勢呈現全線上揚 ， 但
因市場對升息預期押注變化 ， 美國短天期殖
利率上揚幅度更加劇烈 ， 使殖利率曲線再次
呈現「趨平」(長、短天期利差縮窄)，雖然
和緩先前市場對成長股未來估值折價的影
響 ， 但卻不利利率類產品價格 ， 尤其是希望
透過買、賣債券獲取資本利得的交易者。

▋ 非投等債表現優於公債、投等債 
在殖利率曲線全線上揚下 ， 各類債券產品在
2026 年的表現自然趨於弱勢，不僅與股權
類商品有明顯差距 ， 甚至多數類別全年呈現
下跌 ， 僅有非投等債維持上漲 ， 反映其相對
公債及投等債存續期間短(對利率風險的敏
感度相對低 )以及票面利率相對高 (基礎收
益率相對高)的特性 。 而在利差表現上 ， 非投
等債利差持續縮窄 ， 不僅挹注全年表現 ， 也
印證全球經濟仍維持相對樂觀及強勁 ， 短期
不太會出現萎縮 ， 甚至處在 「No Landing」。

### Page 4

2026

02-8751-6665 
 www.fubon.com 
臺北市大安區仁愛路四段 169 號

圖三 ： 非投等債表現明顯優於公債及投等債

資料來源：Bloomberg/北富銀整理, 2026/9/9

圖四：非投等債天期短、殖利率高

資料來源：Bloomberg/北富銀整理, 2026/9/9

然而 ， 非投等債利差也非全面性地縮窄 ， 而
是依產業獲利及財務狀態而有所差異 。 以北
美非投等債為例 ， 全年表現最好的產業是能
源及非必需消費，信用利差 (OAS)皆縮窄
199bps，尤其能源業在今年油價高漲帶動
裂解價差創新高下 ， 信用利差已來到低於整
體平均(265bps)的 182bps。 而全年表現最
差 的 則是 運 輸產 業， 全年 信 用利 差 放寬
434bps 來到 857bps，已高達非投等債平
均的 3.23 倍。與之相比，北美投等債的信
用利差在去年來到相對低位後 ， 即便今年美
國經濟相對強勢 ， 短期再次大幅縮窄的空間
也相對有限，整體平均由 77bps 微幅上升
至 79bps。 產業同樣以能源產業表現最佳 ，
全年信用利差縮窄 13bps 至 79bps(去年年
底為 92bps)，而表現最差的產業則為資訊
科技 ， 全年信用利差放寬10bps 至 83bps，
充分反映大型科技巨頭為籌措 AI 基礎建設
資金而在今年大量發債 ， 為企業本身財務體
質帶來的負面效應。

▋ 總結 
短期美國政府或難快速解決長期財政赤字
及債務持續擴張的問題 ， 長天期公債殖利率
有逐步走高的風險 。 而目前美國通膨數據即
便有放緩的跡象 ， 但位階仍遠遠高於長期中
性的 2%水準 ， 故即便9 月聯準會利率決議
維持利率不變 ， 市場仍可能維持2026 年內
將升息一碼的預期 ， 將使短期公債殖利率維
持在 4%更長期間。

投資人若希望透過買入債券賺取資本利得 ，
將高度仰賴利差縮窄 ， 而目前北美信用債利
差已在長期相對低位 ， 短期或難再次大幅下
行 ， 故全年收益仍預期以息收為主 。 建議投
資人可買進非投等債 ETF 或基金，將資金
交給專業投資機構布局 ， 分散個別企業風險 ，
鎖定較高的殖利率 ， 即便短期仍有利率風險
干擾 ， 但整體報酬預期將比公債及投等債佳 。

## Document 496: 国債優遇は誰のためか (3) ～「個人向け国債プラス」は法人の預金・通常の国債を動かすか～

Source PDF: all report pdf/国債優遇は誰のためか (3) ～「個人向け国債プラス」は法人の預金・通常の国債を動かすか～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-05
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

My Opinion

2026 年 10 月 5 日

1/4 
国債優遇は誰のためか (3) 
～「個人向け国債プラス」は法人の預金・通常の国債を動かすか～ 
専務取締役 資産運用調査部長  安野 淳

（要旨） 
○ 2027 年１月発行分から、 個人向け国債の販売対象が一部の法人 ・ 団体にも広がり、 名称は 「個
人向け国債プラス」に変わる予定だ。商品の基本設計は維持される。元本割れを避けつつ一
定の利息収入を得たい法人等にとって、余裕資金の運用先が増える。 
○ 資金移動は、定期預金の満期や通常の国債の償還に合わせて、段階的に進むと考えられる。
購入規模は、預金金利との比較に加え、各法人の資金需要や運用規程にも左右される。ただ
し、財団等の資産総額をそのまま潜在的な購入額とみなすことはできない。 
○ 預金から国債への資金移動が進んでも、銀行の資金供給力が直ちに低下するわけではない。
制度の評価には、国債の安定保有層の拡大とともに、資金移動の規模や速度、銀行の資金調
達・流動性管理の対応を踏まえ、成長資金の供給への影響を見極める必要がある。

1. 購入者の拡大が生む新たな選択肢 
財務省は、2026 年 12 月募集分（2027 年１月発行分）から、個人向け国債の販売対象を一部の
法人・団体へ広げ、名称を「個人向け国債プラス」に変更する予定だ。金融商品取引法上の特定
投資家制度を参考に、販売対象は「一般投資家」に該当する法人等とされており、学校法人、医
療法人、社会福祉法人、一般社団・財団法人、一定の非上場企業、マンション管理組合などが例
示されている。金融機関や上場企業などの特定投資家は対象外である。個人にとっては主に名称
の変更だが、これまで購入できなかった法人等にとっては、運用先の選択肢が増える（資料１）。 
商品の基本設計は従来と同じである。変動10 年、固定5 年、固定3 年を毎月発行し、1 万円単
位で購入でき、購入額に上限はない。満期には国が額面で償還する。一方、中途換金は原則とし
て発行後1 年を経過してから可能となり、所定の利子相当額が差し引かれる。預金のように随時
使える資金ではないため、日々の支払いに充てる資金とは区別して運用する必要がある。

### Page 2

2/4 
資料 1 個人向け国債プラスの販売対象となる法人等

（出所）財務省ウェブサイトより抜粋

2. 預金からの資金移動は「余裕資金」から 
最初に生じ得るのは、法人等が保有する銀行預金からの移動である。公益活動の運営資金、学
校の施設更新資金、マンションの修繕積立金などには、使途は決まっていても支出まで一定の期
間がある資金が存在する。これまで元本確保を重視して普通預金や定期預金に置いていた部分に
ついて、個人向け国債プラスの新発債の利率が預金金利を上回れば、運用先を見直す誘因にな
る。特に定期預金の満期・更新時は、期限前解約の不利益を避けながら比較できる節目となる。 
安全性の比較も重要だ。利息の付く普通預金や定期預金は、金融機関が破綻した場合、原則と
して一預金者・一金融機関当たり元本1,000 万円とその利息までが預金保険の対象となる。一
方、国債は、預金先の信用リスクとは異なる国の信用に基づく。まとまった額を運用する法人に
は、この違いが選択理由となり得る。ただし、決済用預金は全額保護されるほか、預金には即時
の支払いや金融機関との取引維持という役割がある。預金全体の大幅な流出を想定するのは早計
であろう。

### Page 3

3/4 
3. 通常の国債からは償還・利払いを機に 
もう一つの経路は、法人等がすでに保有する通常の国債の満期償還を機に、再投資先として
「個人向け国債プラス」を選ぶ動きだ。この場合、比較対象となるのは、償還された国債の過去
の利率ではなく、再投資時点で購入できる通常の国債の利回りや保有期間、換金条件などであ
る。変動10 年を選べば、その後の金利変動を利子に反映できる一方、固定3 年・5 年は購入時点
で利率を確定できる。毎年の事業費を利子で賄う財団などでは、必要な収益と将来の支出時期を
照らし合わせて選択することになる。 
通常の固定利付国債を満期前に売却すると、市場金利の変化によって売却損益が生じる。特に
金利上昇局面では、低利率の債券の売却に伴う損失への懸念が、資金移動をためらわせる要因と
なり得る。一方、満期まで保有すれば額面で償還されるため、途中売却による損失を回避し、償
還金を新たな運用先に振り向けられる。 
このため、通常の国債からの資金移動は、一斉に途中売却して乗り換えるよりも、満期償還を
待って再投資先を切り替える形で、段階的に進むと考えられる。利払金についても、事業費など
に充てる必要のない部分は、新たな購入資金となり得る。

4. 販売対象となる法人等の資金はどこまで動くのか 
販売対象となる法人等の資金には、日々の事業や活動に充てるものに加え、将来の支出に備え
て預金や債券、株式などで運用するものがある。ただし、使途や支出時期が定まっている資金も
多く、既存の運用資産を直ちに組み替えられるとは限らない。したがって、法人等の資産総額
を、そのまま「個人向け国債プラス」の潜在的な購入額とみることはできない。 
運用規模の大きい財団では、既存の資産配分や収益目標との整合性が購入の鍵となる。専門の
運用体制を持ちにくい団体にとっては、少額から購入でき、所定の利子相当額が差し引かれるも
のの中途換金時にも元本割れせず、満期には額面で償還される点が魅力となり得る。いずれも購
入資格や内部規程を確認し、将来の支出予定を踏まえて、預金や既存の運用商品から振り向けら
れる資金を見極める必要がある。

5. 資金移動を見極める視点 
資金移動は販売開始と同時に一斉に進むのではなく、定期預金の更新、通常の国債の償還、年
度ごとの運用方針の改定に沿って段階的に表れるだろう。販売金融機関によっては法人向け取扱
いの開始が予定より遅れる可能性もあり、初期の販売額だけで潜在需要を判断できない。比較す
べきは税引き後の利回りだけでなく、資金を使う時期、換金制約、保有債券の含み損益、預金の
決済機能である。 
固定 3 年・5 年は利子収入を見通しやすい一方、将来の金利上昇は反映されない。変動10 年は
金利上昇に対応しやすいが、金利低下時には受取利子が減る。また、法人等が購入した商品は他
者に譲渡できない。事業費や修繕費の支払い時期も踏まえ、保有期間を選ぶ必要がある。 
個人向け国債プラスの販売が増えても、それが預金からの資金移動によるものか、通常の国債

### Page 4

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
4/4 
の償還金の再投資によるものかで、銀行の資金調達や国債市場への影響は異なる。現段階では資
金の出所を十分に把握できず、資金移動の規模や内訳を見通すことは難しい。

6. 銀行の預金基盤と成長資金の供給 
個人向け国債の2025 年度の発行額は6 兆 1,526 億円と、前年度から約1.7 兆円増加した。ただ
し、このうち銀行預金から移行した資金がどの程度を占めるかは明らかではない。販売対象の拡
大に伴い、個人・法人双方で預金から国債への資金移動が進む可能性はあるものの、その動きを
直ちに銀行の資金供給力の低下と結び付けることは適切ではない。 
銀行は、日々の預金の増減に応じて資金繰りを調整しており、預金が貸出を上回る場合にも、
市場からの資金調達を行っている。健全な資本基盤と市場の信認が維持されていれば、預金の減
少に対しても、市場調達や手元流動性の活用を通じて対応する余地がある。また、日銀の国債保
有が減少する過程では、銀行が資産として保有する日銀当座預金も減少している。預金の減少に
資産側の縮小が対応する部分もあることから、貸出への影響は、預金残高の変化だけでなく、資
産・負債の構成や流動性の状況を総合的に捉えて判断する必要がある。 
もっとも、特定の銀行や地域に大規模かつ急速な資金流出が集中すれば、調達コストや流動性
管理を通じて、企業への融資条件などに影響が及ぶ可能性はある。その程度は、各銀行の財務基
盤、資産構成、代替的な資金調達能力によって異なる。政府の「成長投資を促進するための金融
戦略－資産運用立国のアップグレード－」が掲げる銀行等の資金供給・成長支援機能の強化との
関係でも、こうした条件を踏まえた検証が求められる。制度の評価に当たっては、国債の安定保
有層の拡大とともに、預金動向、銀行の資金調達・資産運用の対応、企業向け貸出の状況を併せ
て点検し、国債の安定消化と成長資金の円滑な供給との両立を見極めることが重要である。

以 上

3 
本周热点评论 
1、 美国通胀压力持续，就业降温加大美联储政策权衡难度  
10 月 7 日，美联储公布 9 月会议纪要，以 12 票赞成、0 票反对将
联邦基金利率目标区间上调 25 个基点至 3.75%—4.00%。多数与会者
认为，能源成本、关税及人工智能投资需求带来的通胀风险仍偏向上
行，年内可能需要再次加息，但后续决策取决于经济数据。 9 月 30 日
公布的修订数据显示，8 月 PCE 和核心 PCE 同比涨幅分别为 3.4%和
3.0%，虽低于会议期间估算的 3.8%和 3.4%，仍高于 2%的长期目标。
与此同时，美国就业增长趋弱。10 月 2 日公布的数据显示，9 月非农
就业仅增加 2.9 万人，7—8 月新增就业合计下修 6 万人；失业率升至
4.2%， 私人部门平均时薪环比上涨0.1%、 同比上涨3.0%。 就业与工资
增长放缓， 进一步加大美联储在抑制通胀与稳定就业之间的权衡难度。 
中诚信国际点评： 
此次加息体现了美联储防范通胀压力扩散的政策取向，但随后公
布的就业数据表明，进一步收紧的经济代价正在上升。当前政策难点
在于，通胀尚未充分回落，劳动力需求却已显露疲态，美联储维持价
格稳定与充分就业的政策空间正受到双向挤压。 美联储本次加息主要
体现防范通胀扩散、稳定通胀预期的意图，尚不宜视为持续加息周期
的开启。当前美国经济尚未出现全面衰退迹象，但增长基础正趋于分
化。8 月实际个人消费支出环比增长 0.6%，实际可支配收入持平，储
蓄率降至 4.1%，说明部分消费增长依赖居民降低储蓄率，持续性仍待
观察。能源价格、关税成本转嫁和人工智能投资需求，也可能使通胀
回落更加曲折。对美国主权信用而言，加息有助于 美联储修复信誉，
但政策成效仍待检验；高利率与结构性财政赤字叠加 ，将进一步抬升
政府利息负担，财政实力弱化仍是制约其主权信用的核心因素。近期
美债收益率上升，也反映市场对政策利率路径与长期融资条件的重新
评估。 同时，9 月新增就业放缓， 前两个月数据下修，表明劳动力需求
减弱具有一定持续性 ；工资增速下降则意味着就业市场对服务通胀的
推动减弱。目前失业率仍处于相对低位， 但企业减少招聘通常是劳动
力市场转弱的早期信号 ，可能通过居民收入、消费信心和住房需求加
大经济下行压力。美联储的政策两难由此更加突出： 若继续收紧，虽
有助于抑制通胀预期并降低价格压力固化的风险，但也会进一步推高
居民和企业融资成本，加大消费、房地产投资和就业下行压力。

4 
本周资讯

1、加拿大就业连续两个月下降，内需压力加大  
10 月 9 日，加拿大统计局公布 9 月劳动力调查，就业人数环比减
少 6.8 万人、降幅 0.3%，其中全职和兼职就业分别减少 3.5 万人和 3.3
万人；继 8 月减少 4.2 万人后，就业连续第二个月下降。失业率上升
0.1 个百分点至 6.5%，就业率下降 0.2 个百分点至 60.6%。就业减少主
要集中于 15 至 24 岁青年、25 至 54 岁女性， 以及教育、 医疗和社会援
助、制造业等行业；魁北克和不列颠哥伦比亚省降幅较大。  
10 月 6 日公布的贸易数据则显示，加拿大 8 月商品出口环比增长
2.5%至 779 亿加元，进口下降 2.0%至 737 亿加元，全球商品贸易顺差
由 7 月的 7.87 亿加元扩大至 42 亿加元， 为连续第六个月录得顺差； 对
美国顺差由 61 亿加元升至 112 亿加元。外贸改善为经济提供缓冲，但
就业下降、制造业岗位减少和工资同比仅增长 2.3%，说明国内需求及
企业用工仍偏弱。若贸易摩擦进一步影响订单和投资，加拿大货币与
财政政策面临的稳增长压力可能上升。 
2、澳大利亚央行年内第四次加息，利率升至近 15 年来最高水平 
9 月 29 日， 澳联储货币政策委员会一致决定将现金利率上调25 个
基点至 4.60%，升至约 15 年来最高水平，并释放未来仍可能继续加息
的信号。 此次加息符合市场预期， 是澳洲联储今年第四次提高利率， 累
计加息幅度达到 100 个基点。澳洲联储在政策声明中表示，此前会议
提出的部分通胀上行风险正在兑现。该央行指出，中东冲突扩大导致
全球能源价格明显高于此前假设，同时近期数据显示，澳大利亚经济
增长和通胀表现均强于预期。澳洲联储表示，将继续采取其认为必要
的措施控制通胀，包括进一步调整政策利率。这意味着后续加息并未
被排除，市场将重点关注即将公布的通胀数据以及经济活动变化。  
3、伊朗战争拖累海湾经济， 2026 年或萎缩 5% 
持续时间超出预期的伊朗战争，正在给海湾经济体带来比市场此
前预期更严重的冲击。 英国咨询机构凯投宏观9 月 22 日发布季度区域
展望称，如果冲突迟迟无法结束，海湾经济体 2026 年 GDP 可能整体

经济

差快报

国际宏观资讯周报

5 
萎缩约 5%，降幅接近新冠疫情冲击时期。海湾经济复苏将是一个“缓
慢且颠簸” 的过程。 能源出口至少要到2027 年初才能恢复至战前水平；
与此同时，财政刺激空间有限、利率跟随美联储维持高位以及旅游业

预测可能低估了战争对海湾经济造成的实际损失。  
4、越南第三季度 GDP 增长 9.95%，前九个月贸易转为逆差 
10 月 3 日， 越南财政部统计局公布， 三季度GDP 同比增长 9.95%，
高于一季度的 8.15%和二季度的 8.81%，为 2010 年以来同期最高（不
含 2022 年疫后反弹）；前九个月累计增长 9.01%，其中工业与建筑业
增长 11.21%、创 2015 年以来新高， 服务业增长8.69%。 增长动力主要
来自加工制造、公共投资加快与出口订单，三季度资本形成总额增长
21.39%、货物和服务出口增长 23.27%。外贸与外资结构出现转折。前
九个月进出口总额 8880.2 亿美元、同比增长 30.4%，其中出口 4343.0
亿美元、进口 4537.2 亿美元，累计贸易逆差 194.2 亿美元，而去年同
期为顺差 168.7 亿美元；9 月单月出口 594.8 亿美元、进口 582.1 亿美
元，顺差 12.7 亿美元，结束连续九个月逆差。外资部门（含原油）出
口 3504.1 亿美元、 占出口总额80.7%， 内资部门占比降至19.3%、 增速
仅 7.5%。同期注册外商直接投资 503.6 亿美元、同比增长 76.4%，实
际到位 210.7 亿美元、增长 12.1%，为近五年同期最高。 
5、菲律宾通胀升至 7.2%，创三年多新高 
10 月 6 日，菲律宾统计局公布 9 月通胀率为 7.2%，高于 8 月的
6.1%，与 4 月持平、为 2023 年 3 月以来最高，前九个月均值 5.4%。
分项看，食品及非酒精饮料上涨 6.7%（8 月为 4.6%），大米涨幅升至
20.3%， 交通运输上涨14.6%， 住房水电燃气上涨8.4%， 核心通胀由4.1%
升至 4.7%；大马尼拉以外地区通胀 7.6%，高于首都区的 5.4%。通胀
上行主要由供给端因素推动：热带风暴与季风雨影响农产品运销，国
际油价高企推高运输与物流成本，9 月底实施的运费与最低工资上调形
成叠加。政府已暂停液化石油气与煤油消费税、将电价次级价格上限
扩大至米沙鄢与棉兰老电网、对农产品运输车辆减免通行费，并为厄
尔尼诺应对预留 450 亿比索，其中 62 亿已拨付。 
6、南非央行加息 25 个基点至 7.25%，下调今年经济增长预期

### Page 6

差快报

国际宏观资讯周报

6 
南非央行 9 月 23 日宣布，将政策利率上调 25 个基点至 7.25%，9
月 25 日起生效。 货币政策委员会一致支持加息。 央行同时将今年经济
增长预期从 1.4%下调至 1.2%， 认为燃油价格上涨及服务业价格压力加
大了通胀风险。南非统计局当天公布， 8 月消费者价格指数同比上涨
4.4%，高于 7 月的 4.3%。8 月汽油价格环比下降 2.0%，柴油价格则上
涨 3.1%。南非央行认为，近期燃油价格再次面临上涨压力，预计整体
通胀率将在今年晚些时候及明年初超过 5%，到2027 年底前后回到 3%
的目标水平。央行行长卡尼亚戈表示，政策需要防止持续的价格冲击
扩散到更广泛的商品和服务。央行指出，食品价格及进口价格目前相
对平稳，但服务业通胀仍偏高，长期通胀预期也高于 3%的目标。 
7、尼日利亚央行降息至 23%，货币政策转向宽松 
9 月 22 日，尼日利亚中央银行举行货币政策委员会会议，将基准
利率从 26.5%下调 350 个基点至 23%，创下自 2007 年有记录以来的最
大单次降幅。当地专家和官员认为，此次降息标志着尼日利亚货币政
策向宽松方向发生转变， 也释放出该国宏观经济状况正在改善的信号。
此次降息前，尼日利亚央行曾在 5 月和 7 月将基准利率维持在 26.5%
不变。 尼日利亚央行行长奥拉耶米· 卡多索称， 此次调整主要得益于通
胀率持续回落以及外汇市场状况改善。当地金融领域专家对降息表示
欢迎， 并敦促央行进一步降息， 以扩大中小企业获得信贷的渠道， 支持
工业增长。

1、法国拟削减 430 亿欧元开支，通过增值税和所得税增收  
法国政府希望通过削减 430 亿欧元开支及其他措施，包括增加增
值税和所得税收入，来降低不断膨胀的赤字。预算计划显示， 2027 年
预算案将于 10 月 1 日在议会进行审议，而明年将举行大选，这被视为
迄今为止极右翼寻求夺取政权并取代中间派总统马克龙的最佳时机。
作为欧元区第二大经济体，法国在马克龙于 2024 年宣布提前选举、 导
致议会悬峙后陷入政治危机。此后，两位接连上任的总理均因削减开
支措施而下台。总理塞巴斯蒂安·勒科尔尼只有在同意废除马克龙标
志性的 2023 年养老金改革后，才得以推动 2026 年预算案在下议院获
通过。随着美伊战争推高全球燃油价格，引发民众对生活费上升日趋

财政

不满，勒科尔尼如今面临让 2027 年预算案获批的挑战。预算案称，计
划包括“实施 430 亿欧元的整顿措施”。预算案显示，加上“2026 年
及此前通过的纠正措施” ，2027 年的 “总体努力” 将达到540 亿欧元。
根据计划， 增值税收入将较今年增加逾70 亿欧元， 所得税收入则将增

年增加 180 亿欧元，达到 3750 亿欧元。 
2、澳大利亚 2025/2026 财年赤字录得 223 亿澳元，低于预算预测 
澳大利亚政府 9 月 28 日公布，2025/2026 财年最终财政赤字为 223
亿澳元， 占国内生产总值的0.8%。 这一结果比今年 5 月编制 2026/2027
财年预算时预计的 283 亿澳元少 60 亿澳元。 税收收入高于预期、 政府
支出低于预期， 共同改善了财政收支。 与5 月预算预测相比，2025/2026
财年税收收入增加 46 亿澳元。 澳大利亚政府表示， 主要原因是养老基
金和投资收入带来的税款高于预期。查尔默斯称，收入改善并非来自
工资税或矿业税超预期；矿业利润反而低于此前预测。同期政府支出
比预算预测少 14 亿澳元。财政部长凯蒂·加拉格尔表示，老年护理、
儿童保育和药品补贴等项目的支出低于预计水平。收入和支出两方面
的变化，合计使最终赤字比 5 月预测缩小 60 亿澳元。 
3、新西兰国家党发布大选财政方案，实现持续盈余前不考虑减税  
10 月 12 日，新西兰执政党国家党在 11 月 7 日大选前公布财政方
案，将削减债务和保障医疗、基础设施等一线公共服务列为首要施政
目标，明确在财政账目恢复盈利并实现盈余持续增长前不考虑减税，
以兑现财政整顿承诺、增强抵御外部冲击的缓冲能力。财政事务发言
人妮可拉・威利斯 10 月 12 日表示，当前债务负担与财政赤字状况意
味着减税时机尚未成熟， 尽管财政规划为劳动者预留了潜在减税空间，
但政府须先管控好债务、避免将负担转移至后代，并建立更充足的财
政缓冲；她认为债务水平 下降不仅有助于应对突发经济冲击，也能缓
解利率上行压力。政策文件提出，国家党政府将把实现财政盈余和降
低债务作为当前优先目标，这一立场延续了威利斯任财长三年间通过
削减政府开支、裁减公职人员压降债务和赤字的整顿路径。国家党目
前与新西兰优先党、 行动党组成联合政府， 近期民调支持率低迷， 若要
继续执政仍需两名盟友支持，为换取组阁合作而接纳盟友政策主张，
可能使其财政方案在落地时面临折扣。

1、欧盟延长涉乌克兰危机制裁措施至 2029 年 
欧盟理事会宣布， 将针对 “破坏或威胁乌克兰领土完整、 主权和独
立”的个人和实体的制裁措施延长 36 个月，至 2029 年 9 月 22 日。欧
盟理事会 9 月 22 日发表声明说， 目前相关措施累计制裁个人和实体超
3000 个。措施包括禁止相关个人进入或过境欧盟成员国、冻结相关个
人和实体在欧盟境内的资产，以及禁止欧盟个人和机构向被制裁对象
提供资金或其他经济资源。 欧盟理事会同时决定， 将三名个人、 一个实
体以及另外三名已故人员移出制裁名单。声明未列出被移除的具体个
人和实体。根据欧盟记录，自 2022 年 2 月俄罗斯入侵乌克兰后，欧盟
大幅扩大对俄罗斯制裁范围。截至目前，已对俄采取第 21 轮制裁。欧
盟方面说，相关 措施旨在削弱俄经济基础和能源收入，限制其获取关
键技术和市场， 并继续对俄施加压力。 俄罗斯外交部18 日发表声明说，
为回应欧盟此前批准对俄第 21 轮制裁措施， 俄方大幅扩充禁止入境俄
罗斯的欧盟人员名单。其中包括参与决定向乌克兰提供军事援助的人

3、 巴西总统选举进入第二轮， 财政、 对外关系和环境政策走向面临重
估 
10 月 4 日，巴西举行总统、国会和地方选举首轮投票。巴西高等
选举法院公布， 在99.99%票箱完成统计后， 自由党候选人弗拉维奥· 博
索纳罗获得 5610.43 万票， 占有效票47.03%； 现任总统卢拉获得5387.66
万票，占 45.16%。两人均未取得过半票数，将于 10 月 25 日进入第二
轮。博索纳罗首轮领先幅度超过 220 万票，使原本被视为占优的卢拉
面临更大连任压力，中间派和未投票选民将成为第二轮争取重点。  
大选结果将直接影响拉美最大经济体的财政政策、 国企治理、 贸易
伙伴选择和亚马孙保护议程。卢拉阵营强调社会支出、国家投资以及
与中国等多元伙伴合作，博索纳罗阵营则更重视市场化改革、公共安
全和加强同美国关系。首轮投票后，右翼力量在国会的表现也提高了
下一届政府推进预算、税制和环境立法的协调难度。 10 月 9 日，卢拉
政府又宣布降低燃油税并延长相关补贴，选举前 的价格干预可能缓解
居民负担，也进一步凸显短期民生政策与中期财政纪律之间的平衡压
力。

6、阿比再次就任埃塞俄比亚总理  
10 月 5 日，埃塞俄比亚联邦议会举行会议，成立新一届政府并批
准阿比为新一届政府总理。阿比随后在议会宣誓就职。在今年 6 月举
行的议会选举中，阿比所在的繁荣党获胜。在埃塞 俄比亚联邦议会人
民代表院 （议会下院）486 个席位中， 繁荣党赢得438 个席位。 繁荣党
主席阿比成为新一届政府总理人选。阿比当天发表讲话说，埃塞俄比
亚在进入新的五年发展阶段之际，应从过去的发展历程中汲取经验，
为不断变化的国内、地区和全球形势做好准备。他强调国家团结和共
同发展，表示埃塞俄比亚面临的挑战需要通过集体努力与合作加以解
决。

截止时间均为 2027 年 1 月 12 日。此次程序将评估协定运行、成员履

源和数字服务可能成为下一阶段谈判重点，规则收紧也可能影响亚洲
及其他地区企业在墨西哥和加拿大的供应链布局。  
2、印尼盾一度跌破 18000 关口，资本外流压力上升 
9 月 29 日，印尼盾兑美元一度跌破 18000 关口，雅加达银行间即
期美元汇率报 17877， 盘中最低约18024， 年内累计贬值约7.7%。 印尼
央行将本轮贬值归因于中东冲突下国际油价升至每桶 108 美元以上、
霍尔木兹海峡重开谈判未获进展，以及美国 10 年期国债收益率升至
5.23%引发的资金回流， 并表示已通过离岸无本金交割远期、 境内即期
与 DNDF 交易及二级市场购买国债持续干预。外部账户的结构性压力
同步累积。二季度经常账户逆差扩大至 125 亿美元、相当于 GDP 的
3.3%，一季度为 36 亿美元（1.0%）；央行与财政部、金融系统稳定委
员会加强协调， 重点推进出口收入留境内规定的落实。 为稳定汇率， 央
行在 9 月 22—23 日议息会议上连续第三次维持基准利率 5.75%不变，
并将买入对冲掉期溢价由 12.5%上调至 3 个月 15%、6 个月 20%、12
个月 25%， 以激励外资与出口商结汇。9 月末外汇储备为 1465 亿美元，
约合 5.3 个月进口。政策组合的另一个方向是财政对冲。 9 月 30 日内
阁敲定四季度约 31 万亿盾（约 19.5 亿美元）刺激方案，包括 3324 万
户家庭每月 10 公斤大米援助（17.5 万亿盾）、1330 万工人工资补贴
（12 万亿盾） 以及扩大第21 条个税减免至约 750 万工人。 这一安排指
向在利率暂不上移的前提下以财政托底增长。但补贴与福利支出刚性
上升，已使 2026 年赤字率预计接近 2.85%、逼近 3%法定上限，汇率
与经常账户压力又难以由货币政策单独化解。

3、跨撒哈拉天然气管道将进入实质建设阶段  
10 月 5 日公布的信息显示，连接尼日利亚、尼日尔和阿尔及利亚
的跨撒哈拉天然气管道 （TSGP） 项目在2026 年进入新的建设阶段。该
管道全长约 4130 公里， 从尼日利亚天然气产区经尼日尔延伸至阿尔及
利亚哈西•鲁迈勒， 并接入阿方现有天然气输送和出口网络， 预计年输
气能力为 200 亿至 300 亿立方米。 
项目由阿尔及利亚 Sonatrach、尼日利亚国家石油公司和尼日尔国
家石油公司合作建设。三国于 2022 年重新启动项目，并于 2026 年 6
月完成最终可行性研究报告。随后，阿尔及利亚境内管段在阿德拉尔
省阿乌莱夫地区正式开工，标志项目由前期研究进入实际建设阶段。
该项目不仅将增加尼日利亚天然气输往国际市场的通道，也有望加强
三国能源和经济合作，进一步巩固阿尔 及利亚连接非洲天然气资源与
欧洲市场的能源枢纽地位。

可预测性，并为吸引投资和中期经济增长提供支撑。正面展望反映，
欧洲一体化进程继续推进有望进一步强化制度基础，带来更多投资、
资本流入及财政收益。

减弱，政府债务预计持续上升。钻石相关收入下降、财政缓冲自 2024
年以来明显消耗，较大规模财政赤字仍将延续。穆迪认为，近期增 收
减支措施能够减缓、但不足以扭转债务上升趋势，预计政府债务率由

调整和矿业恢复潜力，与收入不及预期、融资条件收紧等风险大致平
衡。 
5、穆迪维持安哥拉主权信用等级为 B3，将评级展望由稳定调至正面

降及非石油经济增长，增强了经济抵御外部冲击的能力。宏观管理改
善也推动政府债务和利息负担下降， 预计2026 年末政府债务约占 GDP
的 46%，利息支出约占财政收入的 23%。正面展望反映，若货币、汇
率政策有效性及非石油经济发展继续改善，主权信用实力有望进一步
增强。

## Document 526: 战略油储逼近纪录低位还要再动用！美国拟提供最多1700万桶原油

Source PDF: all report pdf/战略油储逼近纪录低位还要再动用！美国拟提供最多1700万桶原油.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

本周公布数据显⽰，美国战略油储和储备体系创⽴之初的规模仅相差约 1300 万桶。美国能
源部此次征集企业原油交换⽅案属于今年 3 ⽉ IEA 成员国同意合计释放 4 亿桶油储的部分⾏
动。当时美国承诺释放 1.72 亿桶战略油储，但此后的企业响应交换有限。分析认为，近期
飓⻛相关供油中断等因素可能推动美政府进⼀步动⽤储备。
摘要：
美国战略⽯油储备（ SPR ）已降⾄ 1982 年以来最低⽔平之际，政府仍在推进新⼀轮原油交换计
划。
美国能源部于当地时间 16 ⽇周五提出，拟向市场提供最多 1700 万桶战略储备原油，以履⾏今年 3
⽉国际协调释储承诺，并缓解伊朗战事冲击下的能源供应压⼒。
这并⾮⼀次简单的原油出售。按照交换机制，参与企业未来需要归还借出的原油，并额外交付
⼀定数量的原油作为溢价。该计划可以增加短期市场供应，但也凸显出美国在缓解能源价格压
⼒与保障战略储备之间的权衡。
此前交换响应有限，美国继续推进释储承诺
美国能源部此次供油属于⼀项交换计划，即原油以 “ 借贷 ” ⽅式提供给参与者，并在后续⽇期归

还。交付时间为 11 ⽉⾄次年 1 ⽉。投标⽅须在 2028 年⾄ 2029 年间将原油连同⾄少 12.5% 的溢价⼀
并归还⾄战略⽯油储备。投标截⽌时间为美国中部时间 10 ⽉ 21 ⽇上午 11 时。
美国能源部表⽰，本次招标采⽤ “ 协商 ” ⽅式，这意味着 “ 政府可就原油交换数量、品质或本次招
标的任何其他条款与投标⽅展开讨论 ” 。
据报道，美国能源部此次重新征集原油交换⽅案，属于美国今年 3 ⽉承诺从战略油储中提供 1.72
亿桶原油的⼀部分。当时，国际能源署（ IEA ） 32 个成员国⼀致同意合计向市场提供创纪录的 4
亿桶应急⽯油储备，以应对中东战争造成的供应中断。
不过，此前的交换计划执⾏并不顺利。今年 6 ⽉，美国曾就相关批次征集企业申请，但由于企业
需要归还原油并⽀付最⾼达 24% 的实物溢价，最终仅有约 50 万桶原油获得企业借⽤。
美国能源部仍在继续推进这项承诺。 9 ⽉底，该部⻔还提出另⼀项最多涉及 4000 万桶原油的交换
计划，显⽰美国正通过多轮操作向市场投放原油，同时要求企业⽇后归还更多原油，以补充储

备。
战略油储处于 1982 年来低位，回补仍是难题
美国战略油储近年来持续受到关注。此前⼤规模动⽤储备，加上后续补充进度，令库存⽬前处
于 1982 年以来低位。
美国能源部本周⼆公布的数据显⽰，截⾄ 10 ⽉ 9 ⽇，能源储备量维持在 2.83 亿桶，与前⼀周持
平，战略油储（ SPR ）规模 1.862 亿桶，为 1982 年 10 ⽉以来最低⽔平。距离该储备体系在⾥根任
美国总统期内启⽤之初、即 1982 年 8 ⽉的初始记录，⽬前的战略油储仅相差约 1300 万桶。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
28 收藏 分享到 :
写评论
图⽚
媒体本周稍早的分析指出，虽然美国战略油储近期库存企稳，但飓⻛相关的墨西哥湾供油中断
等因素仍可能推动特朗普政府进⼀步动⽤储备。
原油交换与直接出售的区别在于，借出的原油未来需要归还，且通常附带额外原油作为溢价。
美国能源部表⽰，这种机制既能在短期内向炼油企业提供供应，也有望增加未来的战略储备库
存。
但交换机制⽆法⽴即改变库存处于低位的现实。随着中东冲突持续影响能源供应，美国继续动
⽤战略储备能在多⼤程度上缓解市场压⼒，以及后续回补能否跟上，将成为观察全球原油供需
和美国能源安全的重要变量。
